# 1. Their / There / They're

## Type

Homophone
---

## Word 1: Their

### Simple English Meaning

Belonging to them.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Uniharuko

### Use

Shows ownership.

### Where to Use

✅ When something belongs to people.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ For a place.

❌ Instead of "they are".

### Structure (if applicable)

Their + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Their is my house.

✅ This is their house.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Their car is new.

Roman Nepali: Uniharuko gadi naya cha.

Meaning: The car belongs to them.

#### Example 2

English: Their teacher is kind.

Roman Nepali: Uniharuko teacher ramro cha.

Meaning: The teacher belongs to their class.

#### Example 3

English: Their books are on the table.

Roman Nepali: Uniharuko kitab table ma chan.

Meaning: The books belong to them.

### Memory Trick

**Their = Heir = Ownership**

If it belongs to someone, use **their**.

---

## Word 2: There

### Simple English Meaning

A place.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Tyaha

### Use

Shows location.

### Where to Use

✅ For places.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ For ownership.

❌ Instead of "they are".

### Structure (if applicable)

There + verb

### Common Mistake

❌ Their is a shop.

✅ There is a shop.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The school is there.

Roman Nepali: School tyaha cha.

Meaning: Shows location.

#### Example 2

English: There is a dog outside.

Roman Nepali: Bahira euta kukur cha.

Meaning: Shows existence.

#### Example 3

English: Put the bag there.

Roman Nepali: Jhola tyaha rakh.

Meaning: Shows place.

### Memory Trick

**There = Here**

Both talk about place.

---

## Word 3: They're

### Simple English Meaning

They are.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Uniharu chan

### Use

Short form of "they are".

### Where to Use

✅ When you can replace it with "they are".

### Where NOT to Use

❌ For ownership.

❌ For location.

### Structure (if applicable)

They're + adjective/noun/verb

### Common Mistake

❌ Their happy.

✅ They're happy.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: They're playing football.

Roman Nepali: Uniharu football kheldai chan.

Meaning: They are playing.

#### Example 2

English: They're my friends.

Roman Nepali: Uniharu mera sathi hun.

Meaning: They are friends.

#### Example 3

English: They're busy today.

Roman Nepali: Uniharu aaja byasta chan.

Meaning: They are busy.

### Memory Trick

**They're = They are**

Expand it. If "they are" works, use **they're**.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Their | Belonging to them | Ownership |
| There | In that place | Location |
| They're | They are | Short form |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. ______ books are on the desk.
2. Put the chair ______.
3. ______ going to school.

Answers:

1. Their
2. There
3. They're

---

## Quick Revision

* Their = Ownership
* There = Place
* They're = They are

---

# 2. Your / You're

## Type

Homophone

---

## Word 1: Your

### Simple English Meaning

Belonging to you.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Timro / Tapai ko

### Use

Shows ownership.

### Where to Use

✅ When something belongs to you.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Instead of "you are".

### Structure (if applicable)

Your + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ You're book is new.

✅ Your book is new.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Your pen is blue.

Roman Nepali: Timro kalam nilo cha.

Meaning: The pen belongs to you.

#### Example 2

English: Your brother is tall.

Roman Nepali: Timro dai/bhai aglo cha.

Meaning: Your brother.

#### Example 3

English: Your bag is heavy.

Roman Nepali: Timro jhola gahungo cha.

Meaning: The bag belongs to you.

### Memory Trick

**Your = Ownership**

Your house, your pen, your bag.

---

## Word 2: You're

### Simple English Meaning

You are.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Timi chau / Tapai hunuhunchha

### Use

Short form of "you are".

### Where to Use

✅ When "you are" fits.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Before a noun showing ownership.

### Structure (if applicable)

You're + adjective/noun/verb

### Common Mistake

❌ Your smart.

✅ You're smart.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: You're very kind.

Roman Nepali: Timi dherai ramro chau.

Meaning: You are kind.

#### Example 2

English: You're my best friend.

Roman Nepali: Timi mero sabai bhanda ramro sathi hau.

Meaning: You are my friend.

#### Example 3

English: You're studying English.

Roman Nepali: Timi English padhdai chau.

Meaning: You are studying.

### Memory Trick

**You're = You are**

Expand it. If "you are" works, use **you're**.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Your | Belonging to you | Ownership |
| You're | You are | Short form |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. ______ my best friend.
2. Is this ______ notebook?
3. ______ very helpful.

Answers:

1. You're
2. Your
3. You're

---

## Quick Revision

* Your = Ownership
* You're = You are
-------



# 6. Sea / See

## Type

Homophone

---

## Word 1: Sea

### Simple English Meaning

A large area of salt water.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Samundra

### Use

Used for oceans and large salt-water areas.

### Where to Use

✅ Water bodies

✅ Geography

✅ Nature

### Where NOT to Use

❌ For looking with eyes

### Structure (if applicable)

Sea + noun/verb

### Common Mistake

❌ I can sea a bird.

✅ I can see a bird.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The sea is very deep.

Roman Nepali: Samundra dherai gahiro cha.

Meaning: Large salt-water body.

#### Example 2

English: We walked near the sea.

Roman Nepali: Hami samundra najik hidyau.

Meaning: Location near sea.

#### Example 3

English: The sea looks beautiful.

Roman Nepali: Samundra ramro dekhincha.

Meaning: Describing the sea.

### Memory Trick

**Sea = Water**

Think of ships and beaches.

---

## Word 2: See

### Simple English Meaning

To look with your eyes.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dekhnu

### Use

Used for vision.

### Where to Use

✅ Looking

✅ Watching

✅ Meeting someone

### Where NOT to Use

❌ For oceans or salt water

### Structure (if applicable)

See + object

### Common Mistake

❌ I sea the mountain.

✅ I see the mountain.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I can see the moon.

Roman Nepali: Ma chandrama dekhna sakchu.

Meaning: Vision.

#### Example 2

English: Did you see him?

Roman Nepali: Timi le unlai dekhyau?

Meaning: Meeting or seeing someone.

#### Example 3

English: We see many birds here.

Roman Nepali: Hami yaha dherai chara dekhchhau.

Meaning: Looking with eyes.

### Memory Trick

**See = Eyes**

You use your eyes to see.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Sea | Samundra | Water |
| See | Dekhnu | Eyes |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I can _____ a star.
2. The _____ is blue today.
3. Did you _____ the movie?

Answers:

1. see
2. sea
3. see

---

## Quick Revision

* Sea = Samundra
* See = Dekhnu

---

# 7. Right / Write

## Type

Homophone

---

## Word 1: Right

### Simple English Meaning

Correct; opposite of left.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Sahi / Dahine

### Use

For correctness or direction.

### Where to Use

✅ Correct answer

✅ Right side

### Where NOT to Use

❌ For putting words on paper

### Structure (if applicable)

Right answer / right side

### Common Mistake

❌ Please right your name.

✅ Please write your name.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Your answer is right.

Roman Nepali: Timro uttar sahi cha.

Meaning: Correct.

#### Example 2

English: Turn right.

Roman Nepali: Dahine tira ghuma.

Meaning: Direction.

#### Example 3

English: She was right.

Roman Nepali: Uni sahi thiin.

Meaning: Correct opinion.

### Memory Trick

**Right = Correct**

Right answer = correct answer.

---

## Word 2: Write

### Simple English Meaning

To put words on paper.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Lekhnu

### Use

For writing.

### Where to Use

✅ Letters

✅ Notes

✅ Essays

### Where NOT to Use

❌ For correctness

### Structure (if applicable)

Write + object

### Common Mistake

❌ Your answer is write.

✅ Your answer is right.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Write your name.

Roman Nepali: Aafno naam lekh.

Meaning: Put words on paper.

#### Example 2

English: I write every day.

Roman Nepali: Ma harek din lekhchu.

Meaning: Writing activity.

#### Example 3

English: She wrote a letter.

Roman Nepali: Unile chithi lekhin.

Meaning: Past writing.

### Memory Trick

**Write = Pen**

Writing uses a pen.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Right | Correct | Accuracy |
| Write | Lekhnu | Writing |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Please _____ your address.
2. Your answer is _____.
3. Turn _____ at the corner.

Answers:

1. write
2. right
3. right

---

## Quick Revision

* Right = Correct
* Write = Lekhnu

---

# 8. Buy / By / Bye

## Type

Homophone

---

## Word 1: Buy

### Simple English Meaning

To get something by paying money.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kinnu

### Use

For purchasing.

### Where to Use

✅ Shopping

✅ Paying money

### Where NOT to Use

❌ For location

❌ For goodbye

### Structure (if applicable)

Buy + object

### Common Mistake

❌ I by a book.

✅ I buy a book.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I want to buy a car.

Roman Nepali: Ma gadi kinnu chahanchu.

Meaning: Purchase.

#### Example 2

English: She bought a dress.

Roman Nepali: Unile dress kinin.

Meaning: Bought something.

#### Example 3

English: We buy vegetables daily.

Roman Nepali: Hami harek din tarkari kinchhau.

Meaning: Shopping.

### Memory Trick

**Buy = Money**

Buying needs money.

---

## Word 2: By

### Simple English Meaning

Near; through; beside.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Najik / Bata

### Use

Shows position or method.

### Where to Use

✅ Beside something

✅ By bus, by car

### Where NOT to Use

❌ For shopping

❌ For goodbye

### Structure (if applicable)

By + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Bye bus

✅ By bus

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Sit by me.

Roman Nepali: Mero najik basa.

Meaning: Near.

#### Example 2

English: I came by bus.

Roman Nepali: Ma bus bata aaye.

Meaning: Method.

#### Example 3

English: The house is by the river.

Roman Nepali: Ghar khola najik cha.

Meaning: Position.

### Memory Trick

**By = Nearby**

Think "beside".

---

## Word 3: Bye

### Simple English Meaning

Goodbye.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bidai / Phari bhetaula

### Use

When leaving.

### Where to Use

✅ Ending conversation

### Where NOT to Use

❌ For shopping

❌ For location

### Structure (if applicable)

Bye!

### Common Mistake

❌ Buy, see you tomorrow.

✅ Bye, see you tomorrow.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Bye, my friend.

Roman Nepali: Bidai sathi.

Meaning: Goodbye.

#### Example 2

English: She said bye.

Roman Nepali: Unile bye bhanin.

Meaning: Leaving.

#### Example 3

English: Bye everyone.

Roman Nepali: Sabailai bye.

Meaning: Farewell.

### Memory Trick

**Bye = Goodbye**

Same ending sound.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Buy | Kinnu | Purchase |
| By | Najik/Bata | Position/Method |
| Bye | Goodbye | Leaving |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I will _____ a phone.
2. The chair is _____ the window.
3. _____, see you tomorrow.

Answers:

1. buy
2. by
3. bye

---

## Quick Revision

* Buy = Kinnu
* By = Najik/Bata
* Bye = Goodbye

---

# 9. One / Won

## Type

Homophone

---

## Word 1: One

### Simple English Meaning

Number 1.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Ek

### Use

Counting.

### Where to Use

✅ Numbers

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Winning competitions

### Structure (if applicable)

One + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ I won apple.

✅ I have one apple.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I have one pen.

Roman Nepali: Masanga ek kalam cha.

Meaning: Number.

#### Example 2

English: One student is absent.

Roman Nepali: Ek jana bidyarthi absent cha.

Meaning: Counting.

#### Example 3

English: One day I will travel.

Roman Nepali: Ek din ma yatra garchu.

Meaning: Single day.

### Memory Trick

**One = 1**

Think of the number.

---

## Word 2: Won

### Simple English Meaning

Past tense of win.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Jityo

### Use

For victory.

### Where to Use

✅ Competitions

✅ Games

### Where NOT to Use

❌ For counting

### Structure (if applicable)

Won + object

### Common Mistake

❌ I one the match.

✅ I won the match.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: We won the game.

Roman Nepali: Hami le khel jityau.

Meaning: Victory.

#### Example 2

English: She won a prize.

Roman Nepali: Unile puraskar jitin.

Meaning: Got prize.

#### Example 3

English: They won yesterday.

Roman Nepali: Uniharu hijo jite.

Meaning: Success.

### Memory Trick

**Won = Win**

Won is past of win.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| One | Ek | Number |
| Won | Jityo | Victory |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. She _____ the race.
2. I have _____ brother.
3. We _____ the match.

Answers:

1. won
2. one
3. won

---

## Quick Revision

* One = Number 1
* Won = Past of Win

---

# 10. Week / Weak

## Type

Homophone

---

## Word 1: Week

### Simple English Meaning

Seven days.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Hapta

### Use

Time period.

### Where to Use

✅ Calendar

✅ Time

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Strength

### Structure (if applicable)

A week

### Common Mistake

❌ I was weak in Kathmandu.

✅ I was there for a week.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: A week has seven days.

Roman Nepali: Ek haptama sat din huncha.

Meaning: Time.

#### Example 2

English: I will return next week.

Roman Nepali: Ma arko hapta farkanchu.

Meaning: Future time.

#### Example 3

English: She stayed for a week.

Roman Nepali: Uni ek hapta basin.

Meaning: Duration.

### Memory Trick

**Week = Calendar**

Think of seven days.

---

## Word 2: Weak

### Simple English Meaning

Not strong.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kamjor

### Use

Strength.

### Where to Use

✅ Physical strength

✅ Weak argument

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Time period

### Structure (if applicable)

Weak + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ I will come next weak.

✅ I will come next week.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: He feels weak today.

Roman Nepali: Uni aaja kamjor mahsus gardaichan.

Meaning: Not strong.

#### Example 2

English: The plant is weak.

Roman Nepali: Biruwa kamjor cha.

Meaning: Lacks strength.

#### Example 3

English: My English is weak.

Roman Nepali: Mero English kamjor cha.

Meaning: Poor ability.

### Memory Trick

**Weak = Not Strong**

Weak body, weak skill.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Week | Hapta | Time |
| Weak | Kamjor | Strength |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I will visit next _____.
2. He feels _____ after illness.
3. A _____ has seven days.

Answers:

1. week
2. weak
3. week

---

## Quick Revision

* Week = Hapta
* Weak = Kamjor
-------



# 11. Affect / Effect

## Type

Look-Alike Word

---

## Word 1: Affect

### Simple English Meaning

To influence or change something.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Prabhab parnu

### Use

Usually used as a verb (action).

### Where to Use

✅ When something changes or influences another thing.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ As a result or outcome.

### Structure (if applicable)

Affect + object

### Common Mistake

❌ The rain had a bad affect.

✅ The rain had a bad effect.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The weather affects my mood.

Roman Nepali: Mausamle mero manobhabma prabhab parchha.

Meaning: Weather changes my mood.

#### Example 2

English: Lack of sleep affects health.

Roman Nepali: Nindra napugda swasthyama prabhab parchha.

Meaning: Sleep influences health.

#### Example 3

English: Noise affects concentration.

Roman Nepali: Halla le dhyanma prabhab parchha.

Meaning: Noise changes focus.

### Memory Trick

**Affect = Action**

Both start with **A**.

---

## Word 2: Effect

### Simple English Meaning

The result of a change.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Parinam

### Use

Usually used as a noun (thing).

### Where to Use

✅ For results or outcomes.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ As an action of influencing.

### Structure (if applicable)

Effect of + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ The weather effects my mood.

✅ The weather affects my mood.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The medicine had a good effect.

Roman Nepali: Aushadhiko ramro parinam bhayo.

Meaning: Positive result.

#### Example 2

English: What is the effect of pollution?

Roman Nepali: Pradushanko parinam ke ho?

Meaning: Result.

#### Example 3

English: The new rule had little effect.

Roman Nepali: Naya niyamko thorai parinam bhayo.

Meaning: Small result.

### Memory Trick

**Effect = End Result**

Both start with **E**.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Affect | Influence | Verb |
| Effect | Result | Noun |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Pollution can ______ health.
2. What was the ______ of the decision?
3. Stress ______ sleep quality.

Answers:

1. affect
2. effect
3. affects

---

## Quick Revision

* Affect = Influence
* Effect = Result

---

# 12. Accept / Except

## Type

Look-Alike Word

---

## Word 1: Accept

### Simple English Meaning

To receive or agree.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Sweekar garnu

### Use

Receiving or agreeing.

### Where to Use

✅ Accept gifts

✅ Accept offers

✅ Accept ideas

### Where NOT to Use

❌ When excluding something.

### Structure (if applicable)

Accept + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Everyone accept Ram came.

✅ Everyone except Ram came.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I accept your apology.

Roman Nepali: Ma timro mafi sweekar garchu.

Meaning: Agreeing.

#### Example 2

English: She accepted the gift.

Roman Nepali: Unile upahar sweekar garin.

Meaning: Received gift.

#### Example 3

English: They accepted the offer.

Roman Nepali: Uniharule prastab sweekar gare.

Meaning: Agreed.

### Memory Trick

**Accept = Agree**

Both start with A.

---

## Word 2: Except

### Simple English Meaning

Not including.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bahek

### Use

For exclusion.

### Where to Use

✅ Leaving someone or something out.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Receiving or agreeing.

### Structure (if applicable)

Everyone except...

### Common Mistake

❌ I except your gift.

✅ I accept your gift.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Everyone came except Hari.

Roman Nepali: Hari bahek sabai aaye.

Meaning: Hari did not come.

#### Example 2

English: I like all fruits except mango.

Roman Nepali: Malai amba bahek sabai phal manparcha.

Meaning: Mango excluded.

#### Example 3

English: All students passed except one.

Roman Nepali: Ek jana bahek sabai pass bhaye.

Meaning: One excluded.

### Memory Trick

**Except = Exclude**

Both start with Ex.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Accept | Receive/Agree | Inclusion |
| Except | Excluding | Leaving out |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I ______ your invitation.
2. Everyone came ______ Sita.
3. She ______ the award.

Answers:

1. accept
2. except
3. accepted

---

## Quick Revision

* Accept = Sweekar garnu
* Except = Bahek

---

# 13. Advice / Advise

## Type

Look-Alike Word

---

## Word 1: Advice

### Simple English Meaning

A suggestion.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Sallah

### Use

Usually a noun.

### Where to Use

✅ Suggestions

✅ Recommendations

### Where NOT to Use

❌ As an action.

### Structure (if applicable)

Give advice

### Common Mistake

❌ He advised me good advice.

✅ He gave me good advice.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Thank you for your advice.

Roman Nepali: Timro sallahko lagi dhanyabad.

Meaning: Suggestion.

#### Example 2

English: Her advice helped me.

Roman Nepali: Unko sallahle malai madat garyo.

Meaning: Useful suggestion.

#### Example 3

English: I need some advice.

Roman Nepali: Malai kehi sallah chahinchha.

Meaning: Need guidance.

### Memory Trick

**Advice = Thing**

It is a suggestion.

---

## Word 2: Advise

### Simple English Meaning

To give advice.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Sallah dinu

### Use

Usually a verb.

### Where to Use

✅ Giving suggestions.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ As a noun.

### Structure (if applicable)

Advise + person

### Common Mistake

❌ He gave me good advise.

✅ He gave me good advice.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I advise you to study.

Roman Nepali: Ma timilai padhna sallah dinchu.

Meaning: Giving suggestion.

#### Example 2

English: The doctor advised rest.

Roman Nepali: Daktar le aaram garna sallah dinubhayo.

Meaning: Recommendation.

#### Example 3

English: She advised me carefully.

Roman Nepali: Unile malai dhyanpurbak sallah din.

Meaning: Guidance.

### Memory Trick

**Advise = Action**

It is something you do.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Advice | Suggestion | Noun |
| Advise | Give suggestion | Verb |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Thank you for your ______.
2. I ______ you to save money.
3. The teacher gave useful ______.

Answers:

1. advice
2. advise
3. advice

---

## Quick Revision

* Advice = Sallah
* Advise = Sallah dinu

---

# 14. Practice / Practise

## Type

Look-Alike Word

---

## Word 1: Practice

### Simple English Meaning

Repeated exercise to improve skill.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Abhyas

### Use

Usually a noun (British English).

### Where to Use

✅ As a thing.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ As a verb in British English.

### Structure (if applicable)

Practice makes perfect.

### Common Mistake

❌ I practice football every day. (British English rule)

✅ I practise football every day.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Practice is important.

Roman Nepali: Abhyas mahatwapurna cha.

Meaning: Skill improvement.

#### Example 2

English: Daily practice helps.

Roman Nepali: Dainik abhyasle madat garchha.

Meaning: Repetition.

#### Example 3

English: Singing practice improved her voice.

Roman Nepali: Gaanako abhyasle awaj sudharyo.

Meaning: Training.

### Memory Trick

**Practice = Noun**

"C" for concept/thing.

---

## Word 2: Practise

### Simple English Meaning

To do repeatedly to improve.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Abhyas garnu

### Use

Verb in British English.

### Where to Use

✅ Action.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ As a noun.

### Structure (if applicable)

Practise + activity

### Common Mistake

❌ My practise is good.

✅ My practice is good.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I practise English daily.

Roman Nepali: Ma dainik English abhyas garchu.

Meaning: Action.

#### Example 2

English: She practises piano.

Roman Nepali: Uni piano abhyas garchhin.

Meaning: Repeated action.

#### Example 3

English: We practise speaking.

Roman Nepali: Hami bolne abhyas garchhau.

Meaning: Improving skill.

### Memory Trick

**Practise = Verb**

"S" for something you do.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Practice | Abhyas | Noun |
| Practise | Abhyas garnu | Verb |

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Daily ______ improves skill.
2. I ______ English every day.
3. Speaking ______ is important.

Answers:

1. practice
2. practise
3. practice

---

## Quick Revision

* Practice = Noun
* Practise = Verb

# 15. Loose / Lose

## Type

Look-Alike Word

---

## Word 1: Loose

### Simple English Meaning

Not tight.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Khukulo

### Use

For clothes, ropes, screws, etc.

### Where to Use

✅ Not tight.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Losing something.

### Structure (if applicable)

Loose + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ I don't want to loose money.

✅ I don't want to lose money.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: This shirt is loose.

Roman Nepali: Yo shirt khukulo cha.

Meaning: Not tight.

#### Example 2

English: The rope is loose.

Roman Nepali: Dori khukulo cha.

Meaning: Not firm.

#### Example 3

English: My shoe feels loose.

Roman Nepali: Mero jutta khukulo lagchha.

Meaning: Not fitting tightly.

### Memory Trick

**Loose = Extra O = Extra space**

Not tight.

---

## Word 2: Lose

### Simple English Meaning

To not keep; to fail to win.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Gumaunu / Harnu

### Use

Losing objects, games, chances.

### Where to Use

✅ Lose money

✅ Lose keys

✅ Lose a match

### Where NOT to Use

❌ For something not tight.

### Structure (if applicable)

Lose + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ My shirt is lose.

✅ My shirt is loose.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I don't want to lose my phone.

Roman Nepali: Ma mero phone gumauna chahanna.

Meaning: Misplace.

#### Example 2

English: They lost the match.

Roman Nepali: Uniharule match hare.

Meaning: Defeat.

#### Example 3

English: She lost her wallet.

Roman Nepali: Unko purse harayo.

Meaning: Missing item.

### Memory Trick

**Lose = Loss**

Both relate to losing.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Loose | Khukulo | Not tight |
| Lose | Gumaunu/Harnu | Missing or defeat |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. The rope is ______.
2. I don't want to ______ my bag.
3. They ______ the game.

Answers:

1. loose
2. lose
3. lost

---

## Quick Revision

* Loose = Not tight
* Lose = Gumaunu/Harnu
-------------




# 16. Quiet / Quite

## Type

Look-Alike Word

---

## Word 1: Quiet

### Simple English Meaning

Making little or no noise.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Shanta / Chupchap

### Use

Used for silence or low noise.

### Where to Use

✅ Quiet room

✅ Quiet person

✅ Quiet place

### Where NOT to Use

❌ To mean "very" or "fairly"

### Structure (if applicable)

Quiet + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ The movie was quite.

✅ The movie was quiet. (if talking about noise)

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Please be quiet.

Roman Nepali: Kripaya chup lag.

Meaning: Do not make noise.

#### Example 2

English: The library is quiet.

Roman Nepali: Pustakalaya shanta cha.

Meaning: Little noise.

#### Example 3

English: She is a quiet girl.

Roman Nepali: Uni shanta keti hun.

Meaning: Calm person.

### Memory Trick

**Quiet = Silence**

Think of a quiet classroom.

---

## Word 2: Quite

### Simple English Meaning

Fairly; very.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dherai / Nikai

### Use

Used to increase meaning.

### Where to Use

✅ Quite good

✅ Quite happy

✅ Quite interesting

### Where NOT to Use

❌ For silence

### Structure (if applicable)

Quite + adjective

### Common Mistake

❌ Please be quite.

✅ Please be quiet.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The test was quite easy.

Roman Nepali: Pariksha nikai sajilo thiyo.

Meaning: Fairly easy.

#### Example 2

English: She is quite smart.

Roman Nepali: Uni nikai hosiyar chan.

Meaning: Very smart.

#### Example 3

English: I am quite tired.

Roman Nepali: Ma nikai thakeko chu.

Meaning: Fairly tired.

### Memory Trick

**Quite = Very**

Think "quite good = very good".

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Quiet | Shanta | No noise |
| Quite | Nikai/Dherai | Degree |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Please stay ______.
2. The book is ______ interesting.
3. The room was very ______.

Answers:

1. quiet
2. quite
3. quiet

---

## Quick Revision

* Quiet = Shanta
* Quite = Nikai/Dherai

# 17. Then / Than

## Type

Look-Alike Word

---

## Word 1: Then

### Simple English Meaning

At that time; next.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Tyaspachi

### Use

Used for time and sequence.

### Where to Use

✅ First... then...

✅ At that time

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Comparisons

### Structure (if applicable)

First..., then...

### Common Mistake

❌ She is taller then me.

✅ She is taller than me.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Eat first, then study.

Roman Nepali: Pahila kha, tyaspachi padha.

Meaning: Sequence.

#### Example 2

English: I was young then.

Roman Nepali: Ma tyo bela sano thiye.

Meaning: Time.

#### Example 3

English: Then we went home.

Roman Nepali: Tyaspachi hami ghar gayau.

Meaning: Next action.

### Memory Trick

**Then = Time**

Think of "what happened next?"

---

## Word 2: Than

### Simple English Meaning

Used for comparison.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bhanda

### Use

Comparing two things.

### Where to Use

✅ Bigger than

✅ Better than

✅ Older than

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Time sequence

### Structure (if applicable)

Adjective + than

### Common Mistake

❌ I will call you than.

✅ I will call you then.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: She is taller than me.

Roman Nepali: Uni ma bhanda agli chan.

Meaning: Comparison.

#### Example 2

English: This book is better than that one.

Roman Nepali: Yo kitab tyo bhanda ramro cha.

Meaning: Comparing quality.

#### Example 3

English: He is older than me.

Roman Nepali: Uni ma bhanda thulo chan.

Meaning: Comparing age.

### Memory Trick

**Than = Comparison**

Think of "greater than".

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Then | Tyaspachi | Time |
| Than | Bhanda | Comparison |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Eat dinner, ______ sleep.
2. He is stronger ______ me.
3. We went home ______.

Answers:

1. then
2. than
3. then

---

## Quick Revision

* Then = Tyaspachi
* Than = Bhanda

# 18. Principal / Principle

## Type

Look-Alike Word

---

## Word 1: Principal

### Simple English Meaning

Head of a school; main or most important.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Pradhanadhyapak / Mukhya

### Use

School leader or main thing.

### Where to Use

✅ Principal reason

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Rules or beliefs

### Structure (if applicable)

Principal + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Honesty is a good principal.

✅ Honesty is a good principle.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The principal spoke to students.

Roman Nepali: Pradhanadhyapakle bidyarthisanga kura garnubhayo.

Meaning: School head.

#### Example 2

English: She met the principal.

Roman Nepali: Unile principal lai bhetin.

Meaning: School authority.

#### Example 3

English: Safety is the principal concern.

Roman Nepali: Suraksha mukhya chinta ho.

Meaning: Main thing.

### Memory Trick

"The principal is your pal."

## Word 2: Principle

### Simple English Meaning

A rule, belief, or idea.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Siddhanta

### Use

For moral rules and beliefs.

### Where to Use

### Where NOT to Use

Principle of...

### Common Mistake

❌ The school principle is kind.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Honesty is an important principle.

Meaning: Rule or value.

#### Example 2

English: He follows strong principles.

Roman Nepali: Unile balio siddhanta anusaran garchhan.

Meaning: Beliefs.

#### Example 3

English: This principle explains gravity.

Roman Nepali: Yo siddhantale gurutwakarshan bujhaunchha.

Meaning: Scientific rule.

### Memory Trick

**Principle = Rule**

Think of life principles.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Principal | School head/Main | Person or main thing |
| Principle | Siddhanta | Rule or belief |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. The school ______ welcomed us.
2. Honesty is a good ______.
3. The ______ reason was safety.

Answers:

1. principal
2. principle
3. principal

---

## Quick Revision

* Principal = School head/Main
* Principle = Rule/Belief

# 21. Weather / Whether

## Type

Homophone

---

## Word 1: Weather

### Simple English Meaning

The condition of the air outside.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

### Use

Used for rain, sun, wind, cold, heat, etc.

✅ Sunny day

✅ Temperature

### Where NOT to Use

### Structure (if applicable)

Weather + verb

### Common Mistake

❌ I don't know weather he will come.

✅ I don't know whether he will come.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The weather is nice today.

Roman Nepali: Aaja mausam ramro cha.

Meaning: Today's climate is good.

#### Example 2

English: The weather is very cold.

Roman Nepali: Mausam dherai jado cha.

Meaning: Low temperature.

#### Example 3

English: Bad weather stopped the match.

Roman Nepali: Naramro mausamle khel rokyo.

Meaning: Climate affected the game.

### Memory Trick

## Word 2: Whether

### Simple English Meaning

Used when talking about choices or possibilities.

Ki...ki / Yadi

### Use

For decisions or uncertainty.

### Where to Use

✅ Whether or not

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Climate

### Structure (if applicable)

Whether + clause

### Common Mistake

❌ The whether is hot.

✅ The weather is hot.

### Examples

#### Example 1

Meaning: Uncertain.

#### Example 2

English: Decide whether to stay or leave.

Roman Nepali: Basne ki jane nirnaya gara.

Meaning: Choice.

#### Example 3

English: She asked whether I was ready.

Roman Nepali: Unile ma tayar chu ki chaina sodhin.

Meaning: Possibility.

### Memory Trick

**Whether = Choice**

Think "whether or not".

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Weather | Mausam | Climate |
| Whether | Choice/Possibility | Decision |

2. I don't know ______ he agrees.
3. Bad ______ caused delays.

2. whether
3. weather

---

## Quick Revision

* Weather = Mausam
* Whether = Choice

### Structure (if applicable)

Whole + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ There is a whole in my shirt.

✅ There is a hole in my shirt.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I ate the whole cake.

Roman Nepali: Maile purai cake khaye.

Meaning: Complete cake.

#### Example 2

English: He worked the whole day.

Roman Nepali: Unle purai din kaam gare.

Meaning: Entire day.

#### Example 3

English: The whole family came.

Roman Nepali: Purai parivar ayo.

Meaning: Everyone in the family.

### Memory Trick

**Whole = Complete**

Whole = all of it.

---

## Word 2: Hole

### Simple English Meaning

An opening or empty space.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Pwal

### Use

Used for gaps and openings.

### Where to Use

✅ Hole in wall

✅ Hole in road

✅ Hole in shirt

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Meaning complete

### Structure (if applicable)

Hole in + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ I ate the hole pizza.

✅ I ate the whole pizza.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: There is a hole in the wall.

Roman Nepali: Bhittama pwal cha.

Meaning: Opening.

#### Example 2

English: The road has a hole.

Roman Nepali: Batoma pwal cha.

Meaning: Gap.

#### Example 3

English: My sock has a hole.

Roman Nepali: Mero moja ma pwal cha.

Meaning: Torn opening.

### Memory Trick

**Hole = Opening**

Think of a round gap.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Whole | Purai | Complete |
| Hole | Pwal | Opening |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I read the ______ book.
2. There is a ______ in the road.
3. The ______ class attended.

Answers:

1. whole
2. hole
3. whole

---

## Quick Revision

* Whole = Purai
* Hole = Pwal

---

# 31. Borrow / Lend

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Borrow

### Simple English Meaning

To take something from someone and return it later.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Saapati linu

### Use

When YOU receive something temporarily.

### Where to Use

✅ Borrow money

✅ Borrow a book

✅ Borrow a pen

### Where NOT to Use

❌ When you give something to another person.

### Structure (if applicable)

Borrow + object + from + person

### Common Mistake

❌ Can you borrow me your pen?

✅ Can you lend me your pen?

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I borrowed a book from Ram.

Roman Nepali: Maile Ram bata euta kitab saapati liye.

Meaning: I received the book temporarily.

#### Example 2

English: She borrowed my pen.

Roman Nepali: Unile mero kalam saapati lin.

Meaning: She took the pen.

#### Example 3

English: We borrowed money from the bank.

Roman Nepali: Hami le bank bata paisa saapati liyau.

Meaning: Received money temporarily.

### Memory Trick

**Borrow = Bring to yourself**

You take something.

---

## Word 2: Lend

### Simple English Meaning

To give something temporarily.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Saapati dinu

### Use

When YOU give something.

### Where to Use

✅ Lend money

✅ Lend a pen

✅ Lend a book

### Where NOT to Use

❌ When you receive something.

### Structure (if applicable)

Lend + object + to + person

### Common Mistake

❌ I borrowed him my book.

✅ I lent him my book.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I lent my friend a pen.

Roman Nepali: Maile sathilai kalam saapati diye.

Meaning: I gave the pen.

#### Example 2

English: Can you lend me some money?

Roman Nepali: Ke timi malai kehi paisa saapati din sakchhau?

Meaning: Asking someone to give.

#### Example 3

English: She lent her book to me.

Roman Nepali: Unile malai kitab saapati din.

Meaning: Temporary giving.

### Memory Trick

**Lend = Let someone use**

You give something.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Borrow | Saapati linu | Receive temporarily |
| Lend | Saapati dinu | Give temporarily |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Can you ______ me your pen?
2. I ______ a book from the library.
3. She ______ me some money.

Answers:

1. lend
2. borrowed
3. lent

---

## Quick Revision

* Borrow = Saapati linu
* Lend = Saapati dinu

---

# 32. Bring / Take

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Bring

### Simple English Meaning

To carry something toward a place or person.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Lyaunu

### Use

Movement toward the speaker.

### Where to Use

✅ Bring it here.

✅ Bring your book.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ When moving away from the speaker.

### Structure (if applicable)

Bring + object + here

### Common Mistake

❌ Take your bag here.

✅ Bring your bag here.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Please bring your notebook.

Roman Nepali: Kripaya aafno copy lyaau.

Meaning: Bring it here.

#### Example 2

English: Bring me a glass of water.

Roman Nepali: Malai ek glass pani lyaideu.

Meaning: Toward speaker.

#### Example 3

English: She brought her friend to the party.

Roman Nepali: Unile sathilai partyma lyain.

Meaning: Came with someone.

### Memory Trick

**Bring = Come toward me**

Think "bring here".

---

## Word 2: Take

### Simple English Meaning

To carry something away from a place.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Lagera janu

### Use

Movement away from the speaker.

### Where to Use

✅ Take this home.

✅ Take the bag away.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ When something comes toward you.

### Structure (if applicable)

Take + object + place

### Common Mistake

❌ Bring this to school tomorrow.

(when speaker is not at school)

✅ Take this to school tomorrow.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Take this book home.

Roman Nepali: Yo kitab ghar lagera jaau.

Meaning: Away from here.

#### Example 2

English: She took the bag to school.

Roman Nepali: Unile jhola school lagin.

Meaning: Carried away.

#### Example 3

English: Take your umbrella with you.

Roman Nepali: Aafno chatta sangai laga.

Meaning: Carry away.

### Memory Trick

**Take = Go away**

Think "take there".

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Bring | Lyaunu | Toward speaker |
| Take | Lagera janu | Away from speaker |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Please ______ me a glass of water.
2. ______ this package to the office.
3. She ______ her books home.

Answers:

1. bring
2. take
3. took

---

## Quick Revision

* Bring = Lyaunu
* Take = Lagera janu

---

# 33. Say / Tell

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Say

### Simple English Meaning

To speak words.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bhannu

### Use

Focus on the words spoken.

### Where to Use

✅ Say something

✅ Say hello

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Usually not directly before a person.

### Structure (if applicable)

Say + something

Say + to + person

### Common Mistake

❌ He said me the answer.

✅ He told me the answer.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: She said hello.

Roman Nepali: Unile hello bhanin.

Meaning: Spoke words.

#### Example 2

English: What did he say?

Roman Nepali: Unile ke bhane?

Meaning: Spoken words.

#### Example 3

English: I said thank you.

Roman Nepali: Maile dhanyabad bhane.

Meaning: Expressed words.

### Memory Trick

**Say = Words**

Focus on what was spoken.

---

## Word 2: Tell

### Simple English Meaning

To give information to someone.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bataunu

### Use

Focus on the listener.

### Where to Use

✅ Tell me

✅ Tell him

✅ Tell the truth

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Without an object person (usually).

### Structure (if applicable)

Tell + person + information

### Common Mistake

❌ Tell hello.

✅ Say hello.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Tell me the truth.

Roman Nepali: Malai satya bataau.

Meaning: Give information.

#### Example 2

English: She told me a story.

Roman Nepali: Unile malai euta katha sunain.

Meaning: Informed someone.

#### Example 3

English: Please tell him.

Roman Nepali: Kripaya unlai bataau.

Meaning: Give message.

### Memory Trick

**Tell = Person**

Tell someone something.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Say | Bhannu | Words |
| Tell | Bataunu | Person receives information |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Please ______ me your name.
2. What did she ______?
3. He ______ me the answer.

Answers:

1. tell
2. say
3. told

---

## Quick Revision

* Say = Bhannu
* Tell = Bataunu

---

# 34. Speak / Talk

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Speak

### Simple English Meaning

To say words; use a language.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bolnu

### Use

More formal than talk.

### Where to Use

✅ Speak English

✅ Speak to someone

### Where NOT to Use

Speak + language

Speak to + person

### Common Mistake

#### Example 1

English: I speak English.

Meaning: Use a language.

#### Example 2

English: May I speak to the manager?

Meaning: Formal conversation.

#### Example 3

English: She speaks clearly.

Roman Nepali: Uni spashta bolchhin.

Meaning: Speaking ability.

### Memory Trick

**Speak = Language**

## Word 2: Talk

### Simple English Meaning

To have a conversation.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kura garnu

### Use

Informal communication.

### Where to Use

### Where NOT to Use

### Structure (if applicable)

Talk to/with + person

### Common Mistake

#### Example 1

English: We talked for an hour.

English: Talk to your teacher.

Meaning: Communicate.

#### Example 3

English: They talked about sports.

Roman Nepali: Uniharule khelkudko barema kura gare.

Meaning: Discussion.

### Memory Trick

**Talk = Conversation**

People talk together.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Speak | Bolnu | Language/Formal |
| Talk | Kura garnu | Conversation |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I can ______ English.
2. We ______ for two hours.
3. May I ______ to the principal?

Answers:

2. talked
3. speak

---

## Quick Revision

* Speak = Language/Formal
* Talk = Conversation

# 35. Look / Watch / See

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Look

### Simple English Meaning

To direct your eyes toward something.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Hernu

### Use

Intentional action.

### Where to Use

✅ Look at the board.

✅ Look here.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Long viewing like TV programs.

### Structure (if applicable)

Look at + object

### Common Mistake

❌ Look the picture.

✅ Look at the picture.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Look at the sky.

Roman Nepali: Aakash tira hera.

Meaning: Direct your eyes.

#### Example 2

English: Look at this photo.

Roman Nepali: Yo photo hera.

Meaning: Observe.

#### Example 3

English: Please look carefully.

Roman Nepali: Kripaya dhyan diyera hera.

Meaning: Pay attention.

### Memory Trick

**Look = Turn your eyes**

Short action.

---

## Word 2: Watch

### Simple English Meaning

To look at something for some time.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Herdai basnu

### Use

For moving things.

### Where to Use

✅ Watch TV

✅ Watch a game

✅ Watch a movie

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Quick glance.

### Structure (if applicable)

Watch + object

### Common Mistake

❌ I watched at TV.

✅ I watched TV.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: We watched a movie.

Roman Nepali: Hami le film heriyau.

Meaning: Long viewing.

#### Example 2

English: He watches football.

Roman Nepali: Uni football herchhan.

Meaning: Following action.

#### Example 3

English: Watch the children.

Roman Nepali: Bachchaharulai hera.

Meaning: Observe carefully.

### Memory Trick

**Watch = Time**

Longer viewing.

---

## Word 3: See

### Simple English Meaning

To notice with your eyes.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dekhnu

### Use

Natural vision.

### Where to Use

✅ I see a bird.

✅ Can you see it?

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Intentional looking action.

### Structure (if applicable)

See + object

### Common Mistake

❌ See at the board.

✅ Look at the board.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I can see a mountain.

Roman Nepali: Ma pahad dekhna sakchu.

Meaning: Vision.

#### Example 2

English: Did you see him?

Roman Nepali: Timi le unlai dekhyau?

Meaning: Notice someone.

#### Example 3

English: I see many stars.

Roman Nepali: Ma dherai tara dekhchu.

Meaning: Natural sight.

### Memory Trick

**See = Eyes naturally notice**

No effort needed.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Look | Hernu | Direct eyes |
| Watch | Herdai basnu | Long viewing |
| See | Dekhnu | Natural vision |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. ______ at the board.
2. We ______ a movie yesterday.
3. I can ______ the moon.

Answers:

1. Look
2. watched
3. see

---

## Quick Revision

* Look = Direct eyes
* Watch = Long viewing
* See = Natural vision

# 36. Raise / Rise

Confusing Verb

---

### Simple English Meaning

To lift something up.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Uthaaunu

### Use

### Where to Use

✅ Raise your hand

✅ Raise a flag

✅ Raise money

### Where NOT to Use

### Structure (if applicable)

Raise + object

### Common Mistake

❌ The sun raises in the east.

✅ The sun rises in the east.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Raise your hand.

Roman Nepali: Aafno haat uthau.

Meaning: Lift your hand.

#### Example 2

English: They raised the flag.

Roman Nepali: Uniharule jhanda uthaye.

Meaning: Lifted the flag.

#### Example 3

English: We raised money for charity.

Roman Nepali: Hami le sahayogko lagi paisa uthayau.

Meaning: Collected money.

### Memory Trick

**Raise = Requires an Object**

## Word 2: Rise

### Simple English Meaning

To go up.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Mathi uthnu

### Use

Something goes up by itself.

### Where to Use

✅ Sun rises

✅ Prices rise

✅ Smoke rises

### Where NOT to Use

### Structure (if applicable)

Subject + rise

### Common Mistake

#### Example 1

English: The sun rises in the east.

Meaning: Increasing.

#### Example 3

English: Smoke rises into the air.

Meaning: Moves upward.

### Memory Trick

**Rise = Goes up itself**

No object needed.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Raise | Uthaaunu | Lift something |
| Rise | Uthnu | Go up naturally |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Please ______ your hand.
2. The sun ______ every morning.
3. They ______ funds for the project.

2. rises
3. raised

---

## Quick Revision

* Raise = Lift something
* Rise = Go up naturally

Confusing Verb

---

To put something down.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Rakhnu

### Use

Put an object somewhere.

### Where to Use

❌ Resting your own body.

### Structure (if applicable)

Lay + object

### Common Mistake

❌ I want to lay down and sleep. (common mistake)

✅ I want to lie down and sleep.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Lay the book on the desk.

Roman Nepali: Kitab desk ma rakha.

Meaning: Put object down.

#### Example 2

English: She laid the keys on the table.

Roman Nepali: Unile saacho table ma rakhin.

Meaning: Placed something.

#### Example 3

English: Lay your phone here.

Roman Nepali: Aafno phone yaha rakha.

Meaning: Put object.

### Memory Trick

**Lay = Place**

You lay something.

---

## Word 2: Lie

### Simple English Meaning

To rest in a flat position.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Sutnu / Paltnu

### Use

For your body.

### Where to Use

✅ Lie on bed

✅ Lie on the floor

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Putting an object somewhere.

### Structure (if applicable)

Lie + place

### Common Mistake

❌ Lie the book here.

✅ Lay the book here.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I want to lie down.

Roman Nepali: Ma sutna chahanchu.

Meaning: Rest body.

#### Example 2

English: He lay on the grass.

Roman Nepali: Uni ghasma sute.

Meaning: Resting.

#### Example 3

English: The cat is lying on the sofa.

Roman Nepali: Biralo sofa ma suteko cha.

Meaning: Rest position.

### Memory Trick

**Lie = Your body**

You lie down yourself.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Lay | Rakhnu | Put something |
| Lie | Sutnu | Rest yourself |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. ______ the pen on the table.
2. I want to ______ down.
3. She ______ the bag near the door.

Answers:

1. Lay
2. lie
3. laid

---

## Quick Revision

* Lay = Put something
* Lie = Rest yourself

---

# 38. Among / Between

## Type

Confusing Preposition

---

## Word 1: Among

### Simple English Meaning

In the middle of many people or things.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bichma

### Use

For three or more.

### Where to Use

✅ Among friends

✅ Among students

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Usually for only two things.

### Structure (if applicable)

Among + group

### Common Mistake

❌ Divide it among Ram and Hari.

✅ Divide it between Ram and Hari.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: She sat among friends.

Roman Nepali: Uni sathiharuko bichma basin.

Meaning: Inside a group.

#### Example 2

English: The bird was among the trees.

Roman Nepali: Chara rukhharuko bichma thiyo.

Meaning: Surrounded by many.

#### Example 3

English: He is popular among students.

Roman Nepali: Uni bidyarthiharubich lokpriya chan.

Meaning: Within a group.

### Memory Trick

**Among = Many**

Think 3 or more.

---

## Word 2: Between

### Simple English Meaning

In the middle of two people or things.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dui ko bichma

### Use

For two.

### Where to Use

✅ Between Ram and Hari

✅ Between two buildings

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Large groups (usually).

### Structure (if applicable)

Between A and B

### Common Mistake

❌ The ball is among the two chairs.

✅ The ball is between the two chairs.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The ball is between the chairs.

Roman Nepali: Genda dui kursiko bichma cha.

Meaning: Middle of two things.

#### Example 2

English: Choose between tea and coffee.

Roman Nepali: Chiya ra coffee madhye chanaut gara.

Meaning: Two choices.

#### Example 3

English: The road runs between the hills.

Roman Nepali: Bato pahadharuko bichma jancha.

Meaning: Two sides.

### Memory Trick

**Between = Two**

Between A and B.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Among | Bichma | Three or more |
| Between | Dui ko bichma | Two |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. She sat ______ her classmates.
2. The ball is ______ the two boxes.
3. He is respected ______ teachers.

Answers:

1. among
2. between
3. among

---

## Quick Revision

* Among = 3 or more
* Between = 2

---

# 39. Beside / Besides

## Type

Confusing Preposition

---

## Word 1: Beside

### Simple English Meaning

Next to.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Chheuma

### Use

For position.

### Where to Use

✅ Beside the chair

✅ Beside me

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Meaning "in addition to".

### Structure (if applicable)

Beside + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Besides me, there is a table.

✅ Beside me, there is a table.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Sit beside me.

Roman Nepali: Mero chheuma basa.

Meaning: Next to me.

#### Example 2

English: The school is beside the park.

Roman Nepali: School parkko chheuma cha.

Meaning: Nearby position.

#### Example 3

English: She stood beside her friend.

Roman Nepali: Uni sathiko chheuma ubhin.

Meaning: Next to someone.

### Memory Trick

**Beside = Side**

Both contain "side".

---

## Word 2: Besides

### Simple English Meaning

In addition to; also.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bahek pani

### Use

Adding information.

### Where to Use

✅ Besides English

✅ Besides that

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Showing position.

### Structure (if applicable)

Besides + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Come and sit besides me.

✅ Come and sit beside me.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Besides English, I study Nepali.

Roman Nepali: English bahek ma Nepali pani padhchu.

Meaning: Additional thing.

#### Example 2

English: Besides Ram, Hari also came.

Roman Nepali: Ram bahek Hari pani ayo.

Meaning: Added person.

#### Example 3

English: Besides that, I have no idea.

Roman Nepali: Tyo bahek malai kehi thaha chaina.

Meaning: Additional information.

### Memory Trick

**Besides = Also**

Think "in addition".

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Beside | Chheuma | Position |
| Besides | Bahek pani | Addition |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Sit ______ me.
2. ______ math, she studies science.
3. The bag is ______ the chair.

Answers:

1. beside
2. Besides
3. beside

---

## Quick Revision

* Beside = Next to
* Besides = Also/In addition

---

# 40. Farther / Further

## Type

Confusing Word

---

## Word 1: Farther

### Simple English Meaning

At a greater physical distance.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Ajha tada

### Use

Physical distance.

### Where to Use

✅ Farther down the road

✅ Farther from home

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Abstract ideas (usually).

### Structure (if applicable)

Farther + place

### Common Mistake

❌ We need farther discussion.

✅ We need further discussion.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The shop is farther away.

Roman Nepali: Pasal ajha tada cha.

Meaning: Physical distance.

#### Example 2

English: Walk farther down the road.

Roman Nepali: Batoma ajha agadi hida.

Meaning: More distance.

#### Example 3

English: My village is farther from the city.

Roman Nepali: Mero gaun saharbhanda tada cha.

Meaning: Distance.

### Memory Trick

**Farther = Far distance**

Think roads and places.

---

## Word 2: Further

### Simple English Meaning

More; additional.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Ajha thap

### Use

Ideas, information, progress.

### Where to Use

✅ Further study

✅ Further discussion

✅ Further information

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Usually physical distance.

### Structure (if applicable)

Further + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ The park is further than the school. (possible, but farther is better)

✅ The park is farther than the school.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: We need further information.

Roman Nepali: Hamilai ajha thap jankari chahinchha.

Meaning: Additional information.

#### Example 2

English: Further study is necessary.

Roman Nepali: Ajha thap adhyayan awasyak cha.

Meaning: More study.

#### Example 3

English: Let's discuss this further.

Roman Nepali: Yas barema ajha thap chhalphal garau.

Meaning: More discussion.

### Memory Trick

**Further = More**

Think additional progress.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Farther | Ajha tada | Physical distance |
| Further | Ajha thap | More/Additional |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. The hotel is ______ down the road.
2. We need ______ details.
3. Walk ______ to reach the bridge.

Answers:

1. farther
2. further
3. farther

---

## Quick Revision

* Farther = Physical distance
* Further = More/Additional

---
# 41. Few / A Few / Little / A Little

## Type

Confusing Quantifiers

---

## Word 1: Few

### Simple English Meaning

Not many; almost none.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Thorai matra

### Use

For countable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ Few students

✅ Few books

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Uncountable nouns (water, milk, money)

### Structure (if applicable)

Few + countable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Few water is left.

✅ Little water is left.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Few students passed the exam.

Roman Nepali: Thorai matra bidyarthi pass bhaye.

Meaning: Almost none passed.

#### Example 2

English: Few people know the answer.

Roman Nepali: Thorai manchelai matra jawab thaha cha.

Meaning: Not many know it.

#### Example 3

English: We have few options.

Roman Nepali: Hamisanga thorai bikalpa chan.

Meaning: Limited choices.

### Memory Trick

**Few = Not enough**

---

## Word 2: A Few

### Simple English Meaning

Some; a small number.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kehi

### Use

For countable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ A few books

✅ A few friends

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Uncountable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

A few + countable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ I have few friends. (negative meaning)

✅ I have a few friends. (some friends)

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I have a few friends.

Roman Nepali: Masanga kehi sathi chan.

Meaning: Some friends.

#### Example 2

English: A few students asked questions.

Roman Nepali: Kehi bidyarthile prashna sodhe.

Meaning: Some students.

#### Example 3

English: We need a few chairs.

Roman Nepali: Hamilai kehi kursi chahinchha.

Meaning: Small number.

### Memory Trick

**A Few = Some**

---

## Word 3: Little

### Simple English Meaning

Not much; almost none.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Thorai matra

### Use

For uncountable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ Little water

✅ Little money

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Countable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

Little + uncountable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Little books are available.

✅ Few books are available.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: There is little water left.

Roman Nepali: Thorai matra pani baki cha.

Meaning: Almost none.

#### Example 2

English: He has little money.

Roman Nepali: Unisanga thorai matra paisa cha.

Meaning: Not enough money.

#### Example 3

English: We have little time.

Roman Nepali: Hamisanga thorai samaya cha.

Meaning: Almost no time.

### Memory Trick

**Little = Uncountable**

---

## Word 4: A Little

### Simple English Meaning

Some; a small amount.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Ali ali

### Use

For uncountable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ A little water

✅ A little milk

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Countable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

A little + uncountable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ A little books.

✅ A few books.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I need a little water.

Roman Nepali: Malai ali ali pani chahinchha.

Meaning: Some water.

#### Example 2

English: Add a little sugar.

Roman Nepali: Ali ali chini hala.

Meaning: Small amount.

#### Example 3

English: She speaks a little English.

Roman Nepali: Uni ali ali English bolchhin.

Meaning: Some knowledge.

### Memory Trick

**A Little = Some amount**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|--------|---------|------|
| Few | Almost none | Countable |
| A Few | Some | Countable |
| Little | Almost none | Uncountable |
| A Little | Some | Uncountable |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I have _____ friends.
2. There is _____ water left.
3. Add _____ milk to tea.

Answers:

1. a few
2. little
3. a little

---

## Quick Revision

* Few = Almost none (countable)
* A Few = Some (countable)
* Little = Almost none (uncountable)
* A Little = Some (uncountable)

---

# 42. Much / Many

## Type

Confusing Quantifiers

---

## Word 1: Much

### Simple English Meaning

A large amount.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dherai

### Use

For uncountable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ Much water

✅ Much money

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Countable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

Much + uncountable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Much books

✅ Many books

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: How much water do you drink?

Roman Nepali: Timi kati pani piunchhau?

Meaning: Amount.

#### Example 2

English: We don't have much time.

Roman Nepali: Hamisanga dherai samaya chaina.

Meaning: Amount of time.

#### Example 3

English: She spent much money.

Roman Nepali: Unile dherai paisa kharcha garin.

Meaning: Amount of money.

### Memory Trick

**Much = Uncountable**

---

## Word 2: Many

### Simple English Meaning

A large number.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dherai

### Use

For countable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ Many students

✅ Many books

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Uncountable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

Many + countable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Many water

✅ Much water

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Many students came.

Roman Nepali: Dherai bidyarthi aaye.

Meaning: Large number.

#### Example 2

English: I have many books.

Roman Nepali: Masanga dherai kitab chan.

Meaning: Countable items.

#### Example 3

English: Many people agreed.

Roman Nepali: Dherai manche sahamat bhaye.

Meaning: Many individuals.

### Memory Trick

**Many = Countable**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|--------|---------|------|
| Much | Dherai | Uncountable |
| Many | Dherai | Countable |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. How _____ money do you have?
2. _____ students attended.
3. We don't have _____ time.

Answers:

1. much
2. many
3. much

---

## Quick Revision

* Much = Uncountable
* Many = Countable

---

# 43. Less / Fewer

## Type

Confusing Quantifiers

---

## Word 1: Less

### Simple English Meaning

A smaller amount.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kam

### Use

For uncountable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ Less water

✅ Less money

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Countable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

Less + uncountable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Less books

✅ Fewer books

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Drink less coffee.

Roman Nepali: Kam coffee piau.

Meaning: Smaller amount.

#### Example 2

English: We need less sugar.

Roman Nepali: Hamilai kam chini chahinchha.

Meaning: Reduced amount.

#### Example 3

English: She spends less money.

Roman Nepali: Unile kam paisa kharcha garchhin.

Meaning: Smaller amount.

### Memory Trick

**Less = Amount**

---

## Word 2: Fewer

### Simple English Meaning

A smaller number.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kam

### Use

For countable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ Fewer books

✅ Fewer students

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Uncountable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

Fewer + countable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Fewer water

✅ Less water

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Fewer people attended.

Roman Nepali: Kam manche aaye.

Meaning: Smaller number.

#### Example 2

English: There are fewer cars today.

Roman Nepali: Aaja kam gaadi chan.

Meaning: Countable items.

#### Example 3

English: We need fewer mistakes.

Roman Nepali: Hamilai kam galti chahinchha.

Meaning: Reduced number.

### Memory Trick

**Fewer = Number**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|--------|---------|------|
| Less | Kam | Uncountable |
| Fewer | Kam | Countable |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Drink _____ water.
2. There are _____ students today.
3. Use _____ sugar.

Answers:

1. less
2. fewer
3. less

---

## Quick Revision

* Less = Uncountable
* Fewer = Countable

---

# 44. Some / Any

## Type

Confusing Determiners

---

## Word 1: Some

### Simple English Meaning

An unspecified amount or number.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kehi

### Use

Mostly in positive sentences.

### Where to Use

✅ Some books

✅ Some water

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Most negative sentences

### Structure (if applicable)

Some + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ I don't have some money.

✅ I don't have any money.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I bought some apples.

Roman Nepali: Maile kehi syau kine.

Meaning: Certain amount.

#### Example 2

English: She needs some help.

Roman Nepali: Unlai kehi sahayog chahinchha.

Meaning: Small amount.

#### Example 3

English: We have some time.

Roman Nepali: Hamisanga kehi samaya cha.

Meaning: Available amount.

### Memory Trick

**Some = Usually positive**

---

## Word 2: Any

### Simple English Meaning

An unspecified amount or number.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kunai pani

### Use

Mostly in negatives and questions.

### Where to Use

✅ Any questions?

✅ Any money?

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Most positive statements

### Structure (if applicable)

Any + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ I have any books.

✅ I have some books.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Do you have any questions?

Roman Nepali: Timisanga kunai prashna cha?

Meaning: Asking.

#### Example 2

English: I don't have any money.

Roman Nepali: Masanga kunai paisa chaina.

Meaning: Negative statement.

#### Example 3

English: Is there any milk?

Roman Nepali: Ke dudh cha?

Meaning: Question.

### Memory Trick

**Any = Questions/Negatives**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|--------|---------|------|
| Some | Kehi | Positive |
| Any | Kunai pani | Questions/Negatives |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Do you have _____ questions?
2. I bought _____ fruit.
3. We don't have _____ milk.

Answers:

1. any
2. some
3. any

---

## Quick Revision

* Some = Positive
* Any = Questions/Negatives

---

# 45. Much / More / Most

## Type

Confusing Degree Words

---

## Word 1: Much

### Simple English Meaning

A large amount.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dherai

### Use

Basic amount.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I don't have much money.

Roman Nepali: Masanga dherai paisa chaina.

Meaning: Large amount.

#### Example 2

English: How much water?

Roman Nepali: Kati pani?

Meaning: Amount.

#### Example 3

English: She doesn't eat much rice.

Roman Nepali: Uni dherai bhat khadainan.

Meaning: Large quantity.

### Memory Trick

**Much = Amount**

---

## Word 2: More

### Simple English Meaning

A greater amount.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Ajha dherai

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I need more time.

Roman Nepali: Malai ajha dherai samaya chahinchha.

Meaning: Additional amount.

#### Example 2

English: She has more money than me.

Roman Nepali: Unisanga ma bhanda dherai paisa cha.

Meaning: Comparison.

#### Example 3

English: Give me more water.

Roman Nepali: Malai ajha pani deu.

Meaning: Additional quantity.

### Memory Trick

**More = Comparative**

---

## Word 3: Most

### Simple English Meaning

The greatest amount.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Sabai bhanda dherai

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: He has the most money.

Roman Nepali: Unisanga sabai bhanda dherai paisa cha.

Meaning: Highest amount.

#### Example 2

English: Most students passed.

Roman Nepali: Dherai jaso bidyarthi pass bhaye.

Meaning: Majority.

#### Example 3

English: This is the most important point.

Roman Nepali: Yo sabai bhanda mahatwapurna kura ho.

Meaning: Highest degree.

### Memory Trick

**Most = Highest**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|--------|---------|------|
| Much | Dherai | Amount |
| More | Ajha dherai | Comparison |
| Most | Sabai bhanda dherai | Highest |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I need _____ water.
2. She has _____ books than me.
3. This is the _____ useful lesson.

Answers:

1. much
2. more
3. most

---

## Quick Revision

* Much = Dherai
* More = Ajha dherai
* Most = Sabai bhanda dherai

------


# 46. Elder / Older

## Type

Confusing Word

---

## Word 1: Elder

### Simple English Meaning

Older in a family relationship.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Jetho / Thulo

### Use

Used mainly for family members.

### Where to Use

✅ Elder brother

✅ Elder sister

✅ Elder son

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Most objects, places, or non-family comparisons

### Structure (if applicable)

Elder + family member

### Common Mistake

❌ Kathmandu is elder than Pokhara.

✅ Kathmandu is older than Pokhara.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: My elder brother is a doctor.

Roman Nepali: Mero jetho dai doctor hunuhunchha.

Meaning: Older brother.

#### Example 2

English: She is my elder sister.

Roman Nepali: Uni meri didi hunuhunchha.

Meaning: Older sister.

#### Example 3

English: His elder son studies abroad.

Roman Nepali: Unko jetho chhora bideshma padhchha.

Meaning: Older child.

### Memory Trick

**Elder = Family**

Use mostly with relatives.

---

## Word 2: Older

### Simple English Meaning

Having more age.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Badi umarko

### Use

General age comparison.

### Where to Use

✅ Older person

✅ Older building

✅ Older car

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Usually not preferred before close family titles.

### Structure (if applicable)

Older than

### Common Mistake

❌ My older brother. (Not wrong, but elder brother is more traditional.)

✅ My elder brother.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: He is older than me.

Roman Nepali: Uni ma bhanda thulo hunuhunchha.

Meaning: Age comparison.

#### Example 2

English: This building is older.

Roman Nepali: Yo building purano cha.

Meaning: Greater age.

#### Example 3

English: She married an older man.

Roman Nepali: Unile afu bhanda thulo umarko manche sanga bihe garin.

Meaning: Age difference.

### Memory Trick

**Older = General age**

People, things, places.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Elder | Jetho | Family members |
| Older | Badi umarko | General age comparison |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. My ______ brother works in a bank.
2. This temple is ______ than that one.
3. She is ______ than her cousin.

Answers:

1. elder
2. older
3. older

---

## Quick Revision

* Elder = Family relationship
* Older = General age

---

# 47. Historic / Historical

## Type

Look-Alike Word

---

## Word 1: Historic

### Simple English Meaning

Very important in history.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Etihasma mahatwapurna

### Use

For famous historical events.

### Where to Use

✅ Historic victory

✅ Historic agreement

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Any old thing from history

### Structure (if applicable)

Historic + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ This old coin is a historic object. (not always)

✅ This old coin is a historical object.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: It was a historic victory.

Roman Nepali: Tyo etihasma mahatwapurna jit thiyo.

Meaning: Important event.

#### Example 2

English: The leaders signed a historic agreement.

Roman Nepali: Netaharule etihasik samjhauta gare.

Meaning: Major event.

#### Example 3

English: This is a historic moment.

Roman Nepali: Yo etihasik kshan ho.

Meaning: Important point in history.

### Memory Trick

**Historic = History-making**

---

## Word 2: Historical

### Simple English Meaning

Related to history.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Etihas sambandhi

### Use

For anything connected with history.

### Where to Use

✅ Historical book

✅ Historical building

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Meaning "very important"

### Structure (if applicable)

Historical + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ The treaty was historical. (possible, but historic is better)

✅ The treaty was historic.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: This is a historical building.

Roman Nepali: Yo etihas sambandhi bhawan ho.

Meaning: Connected with history.

#### Example 2

English: I read a historical novel.

Roman Nepali: Maile etihasik upanyas padhe.

Meaning: History-related.

#### Example 3

English: The museum contains historical documents.

Roman Nepali: Sangrahalayama etihas sambandhi kagajat chan.

Meaning: Historical records.

### Memory Trick

**Historical = About history**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Historic | History-making | Very important event |
| Historical | Related to history | General history |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. It was a ______ event.
2. We visited a ______ monument.
3. The agreement was ______.

Answers:

1. historic
2. historical
3. historic

---

## Quick Revision

* Historic = Important in history
* Historical = Related to history

Look-Alike Word

### Use

Mityabayi

For things that save money.

### Where NOT to Use

Economical + noun

#### Example 1

English: This car is economical.

Meaning: Not wasteful.

#### Example 3

English: An economical solution was chosen.

Meaning: Cost-saving.

### Memory Trick

**Economical = Saves money**

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Economic | Arthik | Economy |
| Economical | Mityabayi | Saves money |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. The country faces ______ challenges.
2. This bike is very ______.
3. The minister discussed ______ policy.

2. economical
3. economic

---

## Quick Revision

* Economic = Economy-related
* Economical = Money-saving

Confusing Word

English: She did a good job.

Think of employment.

# 52. Trip / Travel / Journey / Tour

## Type

Confusing Word

---

## Word 1: Trip

### Simple English Meaning

A short visit from one place to another and back.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Chhoto yatra

### Use

Short travel.

### Where to Use

✅ School trip

✅ Business trip

### Where NOT to Use

❌ General traveling activity

### Structure (if applicable)

Go on a trip

### Common Mistake

❌ I enjoy trip.

✅ I enjoy travel.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: We went on a school trip.

Roman Nepali: Hami school yatrama gayau.

Meaning: Organized visit.

#### Example 2

English: My business trip was successful.

Roman Nepali: Mero byabasayik yatra safal bhayo.

Meaning: Work visit.

#### Example 3

English: They took a weekend trip.

Roman Nepali: Uniharule saptahant yatra gare.

Meaning: Short journey.

### Memory Trick

**Trip = Short visit**

---

## Word 2: Travel

### Simple English Meaning

The activity of going from place to place.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Yatra garnu

### Use

General movement.

### Where to Use

✅ Love travel

✅ Travel abroad

### Where NOT to Use

❌ A specific trip event

### Structure (if applicable)

Travel to + place

### Common Mistake

❌ My travel was long.

✅ My journey was long.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I love travel.

Roman Nepali: Malai yatra manparcha.

Meaning: General activity.

#### Example 2

English: She travels often.

Roman Nepali: Uni dherai yatra garchhin.

Meaning: Frequent movement.

#### Example 3

English: We traveled to India.

Roman Nepali: Hami Bharat gayau.

Meaning: Went somewhere.

### Memory Trick

**Travel = General activity**

---

## Word 3: Journey

### Simple English Meaning

The act of traveling from one place to another.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Yatra

### Use

Focus on the travel itself.

### Where to Use

✅ Long journey

✅ Difficult journey

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Organized sightseeing

### Structure (if applicable)

Journey from A to B

### Common Mistake

❌ We made a journey around the city. (tour is often better)

✅ We took a tour around the city.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The journey was long.

Roman Nepali: Yatra lamo thiyo.

Meaning: Travel experience.

#### Example 2

English: We started our journey early.

Roman Nepali: Hami le yatra chadai suru garyau.

Meaning: Traveling.

#### Example 3

English: It was a difficult journey.

Roman Nepali: Yo kathin yatra thiyo.

Meaning: Travel process.

### Memory Trick

**Journey = Travel path**

---

## Word 4: Tour

### Simple English Meaning

A visit to many places.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bhraman

### Use

Sightseeing or organized visits.

### Where to Use

✅ City tour

✅ World tour

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Simple movement from A to B

### Structure (if applicable)

Go on a tour

### Common Mistake

❌ My tour to school took 10 minutes.

✅ My journey to school took 10 minutes.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: We took a city tour.

Roman Nepali: Hami le sahar bhraman garyau.

Meaning: Sightseeing.

#### Example 2

English: The guide led the tour.

Roman Nepali: Guide le bhraman chalayo.

Meaning: Organized visit.

#### Example 3

English: They are on a world tour.

Roman Nepali: Uniharu bishwo bhramanma chan.

Meaning: Visiting many places.

### Memory Trick

**Tour = Sightseeing**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Trip | Chhoto yatra | Short visit |
| Travel | Yatra garnu | General activity |
| Journey | Yatra | Travel process |
| Tour | Bhraman | Sightseeing |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. We went on a school ______.
2. I love ______.
3. The ______ was very long.

Answers:

1. trip
2. travel
3. journey

---

## Quick Revision

* Trip = Short visit
* Travel = General activity
* Journey = Travel process
* Tour = Sightseeing

Confusing Word

English: The house has three rooms.

### Use

### Where NOT to Use

Go home

Come home

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Remember

### Use

When you yourself have a memory.

### Where to Use

✅ Remember a date

✅ Remember to call

### Where NOT to Use

### Structure (if applicable)

Remember + noun

Remember + to + verb

### Common Mistake

❌ Please remember me the meeting.

✅ Please remind me about the meeting.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I remember your name.

Roman Nepali: Ma timro naam samjhinchu.

Meaning: I have it in my memory.

#### Example 2

English: Remember to lock the door.

Roman Nepali: Dhoka banda garna samjha.

Meaning: Do not forget.

#### Example 3

English: She remembers her childhood.

Roman Nepali: Uni aafno balyakal samjhinchhin.

Meaning: Keeps memory.

### Memory Trick

## Word 2: Remind

To help someone remember.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Samjhaunu

### Use

When one person helps another remember.

### Where to Use

✅ Remind me

✅ Remind him

✅ Remind us

### Where NOT to Use

### Structure (if applicable)

Remind + person + about/of

### Common Mistake

❌ I reminded the answer.

#### Example 1

English: Please remind me tomorrow.

Roman Nepali: Kripaya malai bholi samjhaideu.

Meaning: Help me remember.

#### Example 2

English: She reminded him about the exam.

Meaning: Helped him remember.

#### Example 3

English: This photo reminds me of my school.

Roman Nepali: Yo photo le malai school samjhaunchha.

Meaning: Brings memory back.

### Memory Trick

**Remind = Help someone remember**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Remember | Samjhinu | Your own memory |
| Remind | Samjhaunu | Help another remember |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I ______ her phone number.
2. Please ______ me about the meeting.
3. He ______ his childhood.

2. remind
3. remembers

---

## Quick Revision

* Remember = Keep in memory
* Remind = Help remember
