English: We discovered the truth.

Roman Nepali: Hami le satya patta lagayau.

Meaning: Found information.

#### Example 3

English: She discovered an old map.

Roman Nepali: Unile purano naksa bhettain.

Meaning: Found something.

### Memory Trick

**Discover = Find**

---

## Word 2: Invent

### Simple English Meaning

To create something new.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Aabishkar garnu

### Use

Making a new thing.

### Where to Use

✅ Invent a machine

✅ Invent a tool

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Finding existing things

### Structure (if applicable)

Invent + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Columbus invented America.

✅ Columbus discovered America.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Edison invented the light bulb.

Roman Nepali: Edisonle bulb aabishkar gare.

Meaning: Created new thing.

#### Example 2

English: They invented a new device.

Roman Nepali: Uniharule naya yantra banaye.

Meaning: New creation.

#### Example 3

English: She invented a useful tool.

Roman Nepali: Unile upayogi aujar banain.

Meaning: Created.

### Memory Trick

**Invent = Create**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Discover | Patta lagaunu | Find existing thing |
| Invent | Aabishkar garnu | Create new thing |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Edison ______ the light bulb.
2. Scientists ______ a new species.
3. Columbus ______ America.

Answers:

1. invented
2. discovered
3. discovered

---

## Quick Revision

* Discover = Find existing
* Invent = Create new

---

# 59. Hope / Wish

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Hope

### Simple English Meaning

To want something possible to happen.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Aasha garnu

### Use

Possible future situations.

### Where to Use

✅ Hope it rains

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Impossible or unreal situations

### Structure (if applicable)

Hope + clause

Hope + to + verb

### Common Mistake

❌ I wish to pass tomorrow. (possible but less natural)

✅ I hope to pass tomorrow.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I hope you are well.

Roman Nepali: Timi sanchai chau bhanne aasha cha.

Meaning: Positive expectation.

#### Example 2

English: We hope to win.

Roman Nepali: Hami jitne aasha garchhau.

Meaning: Future possibility.

#### Example 3

English: She hopes for success.

Roman Nepali: Uni safalatako aasha garchhin.

Meaning: Wants good result.

### Memory Trick

**Hope = Possible**

---

## Word 2: Wish

### Simple English Meaning

To want something different, unlikely, or unreal.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Ichchha garnu

### Use

Unreal or difficult situations.

### Where to Use

✅ I wish I were rich.

✅ Wish you luck.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Normal future expectation

### Structure (if applicable)

Wish + past form

Wish + person + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ I wish it will rain tomorrow.

✅ I hope it will rain tomorrow.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I wish I were taller.

Roman Nepali: Ma ajhai aglo bhaeko bhaye hunthyo.

Meaning: Unreal situation.

#### Example 2

English: We wish you success.

Roman Nepali: Hami timilai safalatako kamana garchhau.

Meaning: Good desire.

#### Example 3

English: She wishes she had more money.

Roman Nepali: Unilai ajha dherai paisa bhaye hunthyo.

Meaning: Unreal desire.

### Memory Trick

**Wish = Unreal desire**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Hope | Aasha | Possible future |
| Wish | Ichchha | Unreal desire |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I ______ to pass the exam.
2. I ______ I were taller.
3. We ______ you good luck.

Answers:

1. hope
2. wish
3. wish

---

## Quick Revision

* Hope = Possible future
* Wish = Unreal desire

# 60. Look for / Find

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Look for

### Simple English Meaning

To try to find something.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Khojnu

### Use

Searching process.

### Where to Use

✅ Look for keys

✅ Look for a job

### Where NOT to Use

❌ When the thing is already found

### Structure (if applicable)

Look for + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ I looked for my keys and found them. (correct)
❌ I looked for my keys yesterday, so now I have them. (not always)

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I am looking for my phone.

Roman Nepali: Ma aafno phone khojdai chu.

Meaning: Search in progress.

#### Example 2

English: She is looking for a job.

Roman Nepali: Uni jagir khojdai chhin.

Meaning: Searching.

#### Example 3

English: We looked for the dog.

Roman Nepali: Hami le kukur khojyau.

Meaning: Tried to find.

### Memory Trick

**Look for = Search**

---

## Word 2: Find

### Simple English Meaning

To get or discover something after searching.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bhettaaunu

### Use

Successful result.

### Where to Use

✅ Find keys

✅ Find answer

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Search process only

### Structure (if applicable)

Find + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ I found for my phone.

✅ I looked for my phone.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I found my phone.

Roman Nepali: Maile mero phone bhette.

Meaning: Search successful.

#### Example 2

English: She found the answer.

Roman Nepali: Unile jawab bhettain.

Meaning: Got result.

#### Example 3

English: We found the missing dog.

Roman Nepali: Hami le haraeko kukur bhettyau.

Meaning: Located it.

### Memory Trick

**Find = Result**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Look for | Khojnu | Search process |
| Find | Bhettaaunu | Successful result |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I am ______ my keys.
2. She ______ the answer.
3. We ______ the missing bag yesterday.

Answers:

1. looking for
2. found
3. found

---

## Quick Revision

* Look for = Search
* Find = Get result

# 61. Borrow / Lend

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Borrow

### Simple English Meaning

To take something from someone and return it later.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Saapati linu

### Use

When YOU receive something temporarily.

### Where to Use

✅ Borrow a book

✅ Borrow money

✅ Borrow a pen

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Giving something to another person

### Structure (if applicable)

Borrow + object + from + person

### Common Mistake

❌ Can you borrow me your pen?

✅ Can you lend me your pen?

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I borrowed a book from Ram.

Roman Nepali: Maile Ram bata kitab saapati liye.

Meaning: Took temporarily.

#### Example 2

English: She borrowed some money.

Roman Nepali: Unile kehi paisa saapati lin.

Meaning: Took money.

#### Example 3

English: Can I borrow your pen?

Roman Nepali: Ma timro kalam saapati lina sakchhu?

Meaning: Asking to take temporarily.

### Memory Trick

**Borrow = Take**

You receive something.

---

## Word 2: Lend

### Simple English Meaning

To give something temporarily.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Saapati dinu

### Use

When YOU give something.

### Where to Use

✅ Lend a book

✅ Lend money

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Receiving something

### Structure (if applicable)

Lend + object + to + person

### Common Mistake

❌ I lent a pen from Hari.

✅ I borrowed a pen from Hari.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I lent my book to Sita.

Roman Nepali: Maile Sitalai kitab saapati diye.

Meaning: Gave temporarily.

#### Example 2

English: He lent me some money.

Roman Nepali: Unile malai kehi paisa saapati diye.

Meaning: Gave money.

#### Example 3

English: Please lend me your umbrella.

Roman Nepali: Kripaya malai timro chatta saapati deu.

Meaning: Requesting temporary use.

### Memory Trick

**Lend = Give**

You provide something.

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Borrow | Saapati linu | Receive temporarily |
| Lend | Saapati dinu | Give temporarily |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Can I ______ your pen?
2. She ______ me her notebook.
3. I ______ money from my friend.

Answers:

1. borrow
2. lent
3. borrowed

---

## Quick Revision

* Borrow = Take temporarily
* Lend = Give temporarily

---

# 62. Bring / Take

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Bring

### Simple English Meaning

To carry something toward a place or person.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Lyaunu

### Use

Movement toward the speaker/place.

### Where to Use

✅ Bring your book here.

✅ Bring food to me.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Moving away from the speaker

### Structure (if applicable)

Bring + object + here

### Common Mistake

❌ Take your bag here.

✅ Bring your bag here.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Please bring your notebook.

Roman Nepali: Kripaya aafno copy lyaau.

Meaning: Move toward me.

#### Example 2

English: She brought flowers.

Roman Nepali: Unile phool lyaain.

Meaning: Carried here.

#### Example 3

English: Bring some water.

Roman Nepali: Kehi pani lyaau.

Meaning: Move toward location.

### Memory Trick

**Bring = Come with it**

---

## Word 2: Take

### Simple English Meaning

To carry something away from a place.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Lagera janu

### Use

Movement away.

### Where to Use

✅ Take this home.

✅ Take the bag away.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Moving toward speaker

### Structure (if applicable)

Take + object + place

### Common Mistake

❌ Bring this to school tomorrow. (if speaking from home)

✅ Take this to school tomorrow.

#### Example 1

English: Take this book home.

English: He took the bag.

Meaning: Carried away.

#### Example 3

English: Take these papers to the office.

Roman Nepali: Yi kagaj office lagera jau.

Meaning: Move elsewhere.

### Memory Trick

**Take = Go with it**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Bring | Lyaunu | Toward |
| Take | Lagera janu | Away |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Please ______ your homework here.
2. ______ this bag home.
3. She ______ flowers to the party.

Answers:

2. Take
3. brought

---

## Quick Revision

* Bring = Toward
* Take = Away

Confusing Verb

## Word 1: Say

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Meaning: Speak words.

To give information to someone.

### Use

Focus on listener.

### Where NOT to Use

#### Example 1

English: Tell me the truth.

English: Tell him to come.

Meaning: Formal speaking.

#### Example 3

Meaning: Use speech.

### Memory Trick

## Word 4: Talk

To have a conversation.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kura garnu

### Use

Informal conversation.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Let's talk.

English: They talked for hours.

Roman Nepali: Uniharu ghantau samma kura gare.

#### Example 3

English: I talked to my friend.

Meaning: Conversation.

### Memory Trick

**Talk = Conversation**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Say | Bhannu | Words |
| Tell | Bataunu | Person |
| Speak | Bolnu | Language/Formal |
| Talk | Kura garnu | Conversation |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Please ______ me your name.
2. What did she ______?
3. I can ______ English.

2. say

## Quick Revision

* Say = Words
* Tell = Person
* Speak = Language
* Talk = Conversation

Confusing Verb

To receive sound naturally.

### Use

### Structure

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Listen | Dhyan diyera sunnu | Attention |

Confusing Verb

English: Please come here.

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |

| Wear | Lagayeko awastha | State |
| Put on | Lagaunu | Action |
| Dress | Luga lagaunu | Person |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. She ______ glasses.
2. ______ your coat.
3. The mother ______ the child.

Answers:

1. wears
2. Put on
3. dressed

---

## Quick Revision

* Wear = State
* Put on = Action
* Dress = Person

# 69. Carry / Take / Bring

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Carry

### Simple English Meaning

To hold and move something.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Boknu

### Use

Focus on holding something.

### Where to Use

✅ Carry a bag

✅ Carry a box

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Direction-focused movement

### Structure (if applicable)

Carry + object

### Common Mistake

❌ Bring this bag all day.

✅ Carry this bag all day.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: He carried a heavy box.

Roman Nepali: Unile bhari box boke.

Meaning: Held and moved.

#### Example 2

English: She carries a backpack.

Roman Nepali: Uni jhola bokchhin.

Meaning: Holds while moving.

#### Example 3

English: Can you carry this?

Roman Nepali: Ke timi yo bokna sakchhau?

Meaning: Move while holding.

### Memory Trick

**Carry = Hold and move**

---

## Word 2: Take

### Simple English Meaning

To carry something away.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Lagera janu

### Use

Movement away.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Take this book home.

Roman Nepali: Yo kitab ghar lagera jau.

Meaning: Move away.

#### Example 2

English: He took the files.

Roman Nepali: Unile file lagera gaye.

Meaning: Carried away.

#### Example 3

English: Take these clothes upstairs.

Roman Nepali: Yi luga mathi lagera jau.

Meaning: Move elsewhere.

### Memory Trick

**Take = Away**

---

## Word 3: Bring

### Simple English Meaning

To carry something toward a place.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Lyaunu

### Use

Movement toward.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Bring your notebook here.

Roman Nepali: Copy yaha lyaau.

Meaning: Toward me.

#### Example 2

English: She brought food.

Roman Nepali: Unile khana lyaain.

Meaning: Moved here.

#### Example 3

English: Bring some water.

Roman Nepali: Kehi pani lyaau.

Meaning: Toward location.

### Memory Trick

**Bring = Toward**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Carry | Boknu | Hold and move |
| Take | Lagera janu | Away |
| Bring | Lyaunu | Toward |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Can you ______ this box?
2. ______ this bag home.
3. ______ your book here.

Answers:

1. carry
2. Take
3. Bring

---

## Quick Revision

* Carry = Hold and move
* Take = Away
* Bring = Toward

# 70. Rob / Steal

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Rob

### Simple English Meaning

To take something illegally from a person or place.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Lootnu

### Use

Focus on person or place being robbed.

### Where to Use

✅ Rob a bank

✅ Rob a person

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Directly before thing stolen

### Structure (if applicable)

Rob + person/place

### Common Mistake

❌ He robbed my phone.

✅ He stole my phone.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: They robbed a bank.

Roman Nepali: Uniharule bank lootey.

Meaning: Crime against place.

#### Example 2

English: The thief robbed him.

Roman Nepali: Chorle uslai lootyo.

Meaning: Crime against person.

#### Example 3

English: She was robbed yesterday.

Roman Nepali: Uni hijo lootin.

Meaning: Victim of robbery.

### Memory Trick

**Rob = Person or place**

---

## Word 2: Steal

### Simple English Meaning

To take something illegally.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Chornu

### Use

Focus on the thing taken.

### Where to Use

✅ Steal money

✅ Steal a phone

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Directly before victim

### Structure (if applicable)

Steal + object

### Common Mistake

❌ He stole me.

✅ He robbed me.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Someone stole my bike.

Roman Nepali: Kasailai mero cycle choryo.

Meaning: Took object illegally.

#### Example 2

English: They stole money.

Roman Nepali: Uniharule paisa chore.

Meaning: Theft of object.

#### Example 3

English: The thief stole a phone.

Roman Nepali: Chorle phone choryo.

Meaning: Illegal taking.

### Memory Trick

**Steal = Thing**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Rob | Lootnu | Person/place |
| Steal | Chornu | Thing |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. The thief ______ my phone.
2. They ______ a bank.
3. Someone ______ money from the shop.

Answers:

1. stole
2. robbed
3. stole

---

## Quick Revision

* Rob = Person/place
* Steal = Thing

# 71. Dead / Die / Death

## Type

Confusing Word

---

## Word 1: Dead

### Simple English Meaning

Not alive.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Mareko

### Use

Describes a person, animal, or thing that is no longer alive.

### Where to Use

✅ A dead bird

✅ Dead body

✅ The plant is dead

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Action of dying

### Structure (if applicable)

Be + dead

### Common Mistake

❌ My grandfather dead last year.

✅ My grandfather died last year.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The fish is dead.

Roman Nepali: Machha mareko cha.

Meaning: Not alive.

#### Example 2

English: The tree is dead.

Roman Nepali: Rukh sukera mareko cha.

Meaning: No life.

#### Example 3

English: We found a dead bird.

Roman Nepali: Hami le mareko chara bhettyau.

Meaning: Not living.

### Memory Trick

**Dead = State**

Already not alive.

---

## Word 2: Die

### Simple English Meaning

To stop living.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Marnu

### Use

Action of becoming dead.

### Where to Use

✅ Die young

✅ Die in an accident

### Where NOT to Use

❌ As a noun

### Structure (if applicable)

Die + time/place

### Common Mistake

❌ He is die.

✅ He is dead.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Many plants die in winter.

Roman Nepali: Dherai biruwa jado ma marchhan.

Meaning: Stop living.

#### Example 2

English: The animal died yesterday.

Roman Nepali: Janawar hijo maryo.

Meaning: Lost life.

#### Example 3

English: People die someday.

Roman Nepali: Manche ek din marchhan.

Meaning: Stop living.

### Memory Trick

**Die = Action**

---

## Word 3: Death

### Simple English Meaning

The end of life.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Mrityu

### Use

Noun form.

### Where to Use

✅ His death

✅ Cause of death

### Where NOT to Use

❌ As a verb

### Structure (if applicable)

Death of + person

### Common Mistake

❌ He death yesterday.

✅ He died yesterday.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: His death shocked everyone.

Roman Nepali: Unko mrityule sabailai chakka paryo.

Meaning: End of life.

#### Example 2

English: The cause of death is unknown.

Roman Nepali: Mrityuko karan thaha chaina.

Meaning: Reason for dying.

#### Example 3

English: Death is a part of life.

Roman Nepali: Mrityu jiwanko ek bhag ho.

Meaning: End of life.

### Memory Trick

**Death = Noun**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Dead | Mareko | State |
| Die | Marnu | Action |
| Death | Mrityu | Noun |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. The dog is ______.
2. Many flowers ______ without water.
3. His ______ was unexpected.

Answers:

1. dead
2. die
3. death

---

## Quick Revision

* Dead = State
* Die = Action
* Death = Noun

---

# 72. Sick / Ill

## Type

Confusing Word

---

## Word 1: Sick

### Simple English Meaning

Not healthy.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Birami

### Use

Common everyday English.

### Where to Use

✅ Feel sick

✅ Sick child

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Usually formal writing

### Structure (if applicable)

Be sick

### Common Mistake

❌ I am illness.

✅ I am sick.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I feel sick today.

Roman Nepali: Ma aja birami jasto mahsus garchu.

Meaning: Not healthy.

#### Example 2

English: The child is sick.

Roman Nepali: Baccha birami cha.

Meaning: Unwell.

#### Example 3

English: She was sick last week.

Roman Nepali: Uni gata hapta birami thin.

Meaning: Ill health.

### Memory Trick

**Sick = Everyday word**

---

## Word 2: Ill

### Simple English Meaning

Not healthy.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Aswastha / Birami

### Use

More formal English.

### Where to Use

✅ Seriously ill

✅ Ill patient

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Usually not for feeling like vomiting

### Structure (if applicable)

Be ill

### Common Mistake

❌ I feel ill in the car. (possible, but sick is more common)

✅ I feel sick in the car.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: He is seriously ill.

Roman Nepali: Uni gambhir rupma birami chan.

Meaning: Very unhealthy.

#### Example 2

English: Her mother is ill.

Meaning: Unwell.

#### Example 3

English: The doctor treated the ill patient.

Roman Nepali: Doctorle birami biramilai upachar gare.

Meaning: Formal usage.

### Memory Trick

**Ill = Formal**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Sick | Birami | Everyday English |
| Ill | Aswastha | Formal English |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I feel ______ today.
2. He is seriously ______.
3. The ______ patient needs care.

Answers:

1. sick
2. ill
3. ill

---

## Quick Revision

* Sick = Common use
* Ill = Formal use

# 75. End / Finish

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: End

### Simple English Meaning

To stop or come to a conclusion.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Antya hunu

### Use

Events, stories, meetings, periods.

### Where to Use

✅ The meeting ended

✅ The movie ended

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Completing a task personally

### Structure (if applicable)

End + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ I ended my homework. (possible but less common)

✅ I finished my homework.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The game ended at 5.

Roman Nepali: Khel 5 baje sakiyo.

Meaning: Came to a conclusion.

#### Example 2

English: The meeting ended early.

Roman Nepali: Baithak chadai sakiyo.

Meaning: Event stopped.

#### Example 3

English: The story ended happily.

Roman Nepali: Katha sukhad rupma sakiyo.

Meaning: Conclusion.

### Memory Trick

**End = Event stops**

---

## Word 2: Finish

### Simple English Meaning

To complete something.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Pura garnu

### Use

Tasks and work.

### Where to Use

✅ Finish homework

✅ Finish food

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Natural ending of events

### Structure (if applicable)

Finish + task

### Common Mistake

❌ The movie finished at 9. (possible, but ended is more natural)

✅ The movie ended at 9.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I finished my homework.

Roman Nepali: Maile homework pura gare.

Meaning: Completed task.

#### Example 2

English: She finished her meal.

Roman Nepali: Unile khana sakain.

Meaning: Completed eating.

#### Example 3

English: We finished the project.

Roman Nepali: Hami le project pura garyau.

Meaning: Completed work.

### Memory Trick

**Finish = Complete task**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| End | Antya hunu | Events stop |
| Finish | Pura garnu | Complete tasks |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. The meeting ______ at noon.
2. I ______ my homework.
3. The movie ______ happily.

Answers:

1. ended
2. finished
3. ended

---

## Quick Revision

* End = Event stops
* Finish = Complete task

# 76. During / While / For

## Type

Confusing Grammar Words

---

## Word 1: During

### Simple English Meaning

At some time inside a period or event.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Ko bela ma

### Use

With nouns (events, periods, activities).

### Where to Use

✅ During the meeting

✅ During class

✅ During the holiday

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Before a full sentence

### Structure (if applicable)

During + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ During I was sleeping.

✅ While I was sleeping.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I met him during the meeting.

Roman Nepali: Ma uslai baithakko bela ma bhete.

Meaning: Inside the meeting time.

#### Example 2

English: She slept during the movie.

Roman Nepali: Uni filmko bela ma sutin.

Meaning: Within the event.

#### Example 3

English: It rained during the holiday.

Roman Nepali: Bida ko bela ma pani paryo.

Meaning: In that period.

### Memory Trick

**During + Noun**

---

## Word 2: While

### Simple English Meaning

At the same time as something else.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Jaba samma / Jaba

### Use

With a full sentence.

### Where to Use

✅ While I was reading

✅ While she was cooking

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Before only a noun

### Structure (if applicable)

While + subject + verb

### Common Mistake

❌ While the meeting.

✅ During the meeting.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I listened to music while I studied.

Roman Nepali: Maile padhda sangeet sune.

Meaning: Two actions together.

#### Example 2

English: She called while I was sleeping.

Roman Nepali: Ma sutda unile phone garin.

Meaning: Same time.

#### Example 3

English: He fell while running.

Roman Nepali: Uni daudda ladey.

Meaning: During an action.

### Memory Trick

**While + Sentence**

---

## Word 3: For

### Simple English Meaning

A length of time.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Samma / Ko lagi

### Use

Duration.

### Where to Use

✅ For two hours

✅ For a week

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Specific starting time

### Structure (if applicable)

For + period of time

### Common Mistake

❌ I lived here during five years.

✅ I lived here for five years.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I waited for two hours.

Roman Nepali: Ma dui ghanta samma parkhe.

Meaning: Duration.

#### Example 2

English: She lived there for a year.

Roman Nepali: Uni ek barsa tyaha basin.

Meaning: Length of time.

#### Example 3

English: We studied for three months.

Roman Nepali: Hami tin mahina padhyau.

Meaning: Time period.

### Memory Trick

**For = Duration**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| During | Ko bela ma | Before noun |
| While | Jaba | Before sentence |
| For | Samma | Duration |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I slept ______ the movie.
2. She called ______ I was eating.
3. We waited ______ three hours.

Answers:

1. during
2. while
3. for

---

## Quick Revision

* During = + noun
* While = + sentence
* For = Duration

---

Confusing Grammar Words

#### Example 3

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Samma

### Use

Duration.

### Where to Use

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Starting date/time

### Structure (if applicable)

### Common Mistake

❌ Since two years.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I have lived here for five years.

Roman Nepali: Ma panch barsa dekhi yaha basdai chu.

#### Example 2

English: She waited for an hour.

Roman Nepali: Uni ek ghanta parkhin.

Meaning: Time length.

#### Example 3

English: We studied for three months.

Roman Nepali: Hami tin mahina padhyau.

Meaning: Duration.

### Memory Trick

**For = Length of time**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Since | Dekhi | Starting point |
| For | Samma | Duration |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I have known him ______ 2018.
2. She worked there ______ five years.
3. We have been friends ______ childhood.

2. for
3. since

---

## Quick Revision

* Since = Starting point
* For = Duration

# 78. Ago / Before

## Type

Confusing Grammar Words

---

## Word 1: Ago

### Simple English Meaning

Before now.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Pahila

### Use

Counting back from the present.

### Where to Use

✅ Two days ago

✅ A year ago

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Reference to another past event

### Structure (if applicable)

Time + ago

### Common Mistake

❌ I met him before two years.

✅ I met him two years ago.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I came here two years ago.

Roman Nepali: Ma dui barsa pahila yaha aaye.

Meaning: Before now.

#### Example 2

English: She left a week ago.

Roman Nepali: Uni ek hapta pahila gain.

Meaning: Past from now.

#### Example 3

English: We met long ago.

Roman Nepali: Hami dherai pahila bhetyau.

Meaning: Long time before now.

### Memory Trick

**Ago = From now**

---

## Word 2: Before

### Simple English Meaning

Earlier than a time or event.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bhanda agadi

### Use

Comparison with another event.

### Where to Use

✅ Before dinner

✅ Before he arrived

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Time counted from now

### Structure (if applicable)

Before + noun/sentence

### Common Mistake

❌ I came here before two years.

✅ I came here two years ago.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Wash your hands before dinner.

Roman Nepali: Khana agadi haat dhou.

Meaning: Earlier than dinner.

#### Example 2

English: I saw her before class.

Roman Nepali: Maile uslai class agadi dekhe.

Meaning: Earlier event.

#### Example 3

English: Finish your work before you leave.

Roman Nepali: Jaanu agadi kaam sakau.

Meaning: Prior action.

### Memory Trick

**Before = Earlier than something**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Ago | Pahila | From now |
| Before | Agadi | Before event/time |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I met him three years ______.
2. Finish this ______ lunch.
3. She arrived an hour ______.

Answers:

1. ago
2. before
3. ago

---

## Quick Revision

* Ago = Before now
* Before = Earlier than event

---

✅ Have you finished yet?

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Positive completed actions

### Structure (if applicable)

At sentence end

### Common Mistake

❌ I have yet finished.

✅ I have not finished yet.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I have not eaten yet.

Roman Nepali: Maile aile samma khayeko chaina.

Meaning: Not until now.

#### Example 2

English: Have you arrived yet?

Roman Nepali: Ke timi aile samma pugyau?

Meaning: Question.

#### Example 3

English: She is not ready yet.

Roman Nepali: Uni aile samma tayar chainan.

Meaning: Not completed.

### Memory Trick

**Yet = Question/Negative**

---

## Word 3: Already

### Simple English Meaning

Earlier than expected.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Pahilai

### Use

Completed action.

### Where to Use

✅ Already finished

✅ Already arrived

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Ongoing action

### Structure (if applicable)

Already + verb

### Common Mistake

❌ I am already waiting.

✅ I am still waiting.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I have already eaten.

Roman Nepali: Maile pahilai khaisake.

Meaning: Completed.

#### Example 2

English: She has already left.

Roman Nepali: Uni pahilai gaesakin.

Meaning: Earlier than expected.

#### Example 3

English: We already know.

Roman Nepali: Hamilai pahilai thaha cha.

Meaning: Existing knowledge.

### Memory Trick

**Already = Completed**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Still | Aile samma pani | Continuing |
| Yet | Aile samma | Question/negative |
| Already | Pahilai | Completed |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I am ______ studying.
2. Have you finished ______?
3. She has ______ left.

Answers:

1. still
2. yet
3. already

---

## Quick Revision

* Still = Continuing
* Yet = Question/negative
* Already = Completed

---

# 80. So / Such

## Type

Confusing Grammar Words

---

## Word 1: So

### Simple English Meaning

Very.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Yeti dherai

### Use

Before adjectives and adverbs.

### Where to Use

✅ So good

✅ So fast

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Before noun phrase alone

### Structure (if applicable)

So + adjective/adverb

### Common Mistake

❌ Such beautiful.

✅ So beautiful.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: She is so kind.

Roman Nepali: Uni yeti dayalu chhin.

Meaning: Very kind.

#### Example 2

English: The car is so fast.

Roman Nepali: Gaadi yeti chito cha.

Meaning: Very fast.

#### Example 3

English: It was so easy.

Roman Nepali: Yo yeti sajilo thiyo.

Meaning: Very easy.

### Memory Trick

**So + Adjective**

---

## Word 2: Such

### Simple English Meaning

Very or of that kind.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Yasto dherai

### Use

Before noun phrases.

### Where to Use

✅ Such a good boy

✅ Such nice people

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Before adjective only

### Structure (if applicable)

Such + noun phrase

Such a/an + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ So a good boy.

✅ Such a good boy.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: He is such a good boy.

Roman Nepali: U yasto ramro keta ho.

Meaning: Strong description.

#### Example 2

English: They are such kind people.

Roman Nepali: Uniharu yasta dayalu manche hun.

Meaning: Emphasized noun group.

#### Example 3

English: It was such a surprise.

Roman Nepali: Tyo yasto achamma thiyo.

Meaning: Strong feeling.

### Memory Trick

**Such + Noun**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| So | Yeti dherai | Adjective/adverb |
| Such | Yasto dherai | Noun phrase |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. She is ______ intelligent.
2. He is ______ a nice person.
3. The test was ______ difficult.

Answers:

1. so
2. such
3. so

---

## Quick Revision

* So = + adjective/adverb
* Such = + noun phrase

-------


# 81. Few / A Few / Little / A Little

## Type

Confusing Grammar Words

---

## Word 1: Few

### Simple English Meaning

Almost none; not many.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dherai chaina

### Use

Countable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ Few students

✅ Few books

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Uncountable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

Few + countable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Few water.

✅ Little water.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Few students attended class.

Roman Nepali: Thorai bidyarthi classma aaye.

Meaning: Almost none.

#### Example 2

English: Few people know the truth.

Roman Nepali: Thorai manchelai satya thaha cha.

Meaning: Small number.

#### Example 3

English: Few books were sold.

Roman Nepali: Thorai kitab bike.

Meaning: Not many.

### Memory Trick

**Few = Countable + almost none**

---

## Word 2: A Few

### Simple English Meaning

Some; a small number.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kehi

### Use

Countable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ A few friends

✅ A few days

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Uncountable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

A few + countable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ A few milk.

✅ A little milk.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I have a few friends.

Roman Nepali: Masanga kehi sathi chan.

Meaning: Some friends.

#### Example 2

English: Wait a few minutes.

Roman Nepali: Kehi minute parkha.

Meaning: Small number.

#### Example 3

English: We bought a few books.

Roman Nepali: Hami le kehi kitab kinyaun.

Meaning: Some books.

### Memory Trick

**A few = Some**

---

## Word 3: Little

### Simple English Meaning

Almost no amount.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dherai chaina

### Use

Uncountable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ Little water

✅ Little money

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Countable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

Little + uncountable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Little books.

✅ Few books.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: There is little water.

Roman Nepali: Pani thorai cha.

Meaning: Almost none.

#### Example 2

English: He has little money.

Roman Nepali: Usanga thorai paisa cha.

Meaning: Not much.

#### Example 3

English: We have little time.

Roman Nepali: Hami sanga thorai samaya cha.

Meaning: Almost no time.

### Memory Trick

**Little = Uncountable + almost none**

---

## Word 4: A Little

### Simple English Meaning

Some amount.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Ali ali

### Use

Uncountable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ A little water

✅ A little help

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Countable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

A little + uncountable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ A little books.

✅ A few books.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I need a little water.

Roman Nepali: Malai ali pani chahincha.

Meaning: Some amount.

#### Example 2

English: She has a little money.

Roman Nepali: Unisanga ali paisa cha.

Meaning: Some money.

#### Example 3

English: We need a little time.

Roman Nepali: Hamilai ali samaya chahincha.

Meaning: Some time.

### Memory Trick

**A little = Some amount**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Few | Almost none | Countable |
| A few | Some | Countable |
| Little | Almost none | Uncountable |
| A little | Some | Uncountable |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I have ______ friends.
2. There is ______ water left.
3. We need ______ more minutes.

Answers:

1. a few
2. little
3. a few

---

## Quick Revision

* Few = Almost none (countable)
* A few = Some (countable)
* Little = Almost none (uncountable)
* A little = Some (uncountable)

---

# 82. Much / Many

## Type

Confusing Grammar Words

---

## Word 1: Much

### Simple English Meaning

A large amount.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dherai

### Use

Uncountable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ Much water

✅ Much money

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Countable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

Much + uncountable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Much books.

✅ Many books.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: How much money do you have?

Roman Nepali: Timisanga kati paisa cha?

Meaning: Amount.

#### Example 2

English: There is not much water.

Roman Nepali: Dherai pani chaina.

Meaning: Quantity.

#### Example 3

English: We do not have much time.

Roman Nepali: Hami sanga dherai samaya chaina.

Meaning: Amount.

### Memory Trick

**Much = Uncountable**

---

## Word 2: Many

### Simple English Meaning

A large number.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dherai

### Use

Countable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ Many people

✅ Many books

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Uncountable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

Many + countable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Many water.

✅ Much water.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Many students came.

Roman Nepali: Dherai bidyarthi aaye.

Meaning: Number.

#### Example 2

English: We have many books.

Roman Nepali: Hami sanga dherai kitab chan.

Meaning: Countable quantity.

#### Example 3

English: Many people agree.

Roman Nepali: Dherai manche sahamat chan.

Meaning: Large number.

### Memory Trick

**Many = Countable**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Much | Dherai | Uncountable |
| Many | Dherai | Countable |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. How ______ water is there?
2. ______ students attended.
3. We don't have ______ time.

Answers:

1. much
2. Many
3. much

---

## Quick Revision

* Much = Uncountable
* Many = Countable

---

# 83. Less / Fewer

## Type

Confusing Grammar Words

---

## Word 1: Less

### Simple English Meaning

A smaller amount.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kam

### Use

Uncountable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ Less water

✅ Less money

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Countable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

Less + uncountable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Less books.

✅ Fewer books.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I need less sugar.

Roman Nepali: Malai kam chini chahincha.

Meaning: Smaller amount.

#### Example 2

English: We have less time.

Roman Nepali: Hami sanga kam samaya cha.

Meaning: Reduced amount.

#### Example 3

English: Use less water.

Roman Nepali: Kam pani prayog gara.

Meaning: Smaller quantity.

### Memory Trick

**Less = Uncountable**

---

## Word 2: Fewer

### Simple English Meaning

A smaller number.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kam

### Use

Countable nouns.

### Where to Use

✅ Fewer students

✅ Fewer cars

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Uncountable nouns

### Structure (if applicable)

Fewer + countable noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Fewer water.

✅ Less water.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Fewer people came.

Roman Nepali: Kam manche aaye.

Meaning: Smaller number.

#### Example 2

English: There are fewer books.

Roman Nepali: Kam kitab chan.

Meaning: Reduced count.

#### Example 3

English: We need fewer workers.

Roman Nepali: Hamilai kam karmachari chahincha.

Meaning: Smaller number.

### Memory Trick

**Fewer = Countable**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Less | Kam | Uncountable |
| Fewer | Kam | Countable |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Drink ______ water.
2. There are ______ students today.
3. We have ______ money now.

Answers:

2. fewer
3. less

---

## Quick Revision

* Less = Uncountable
* Fewer = Countable

---

# 84. Some / Any

## Type

Confusing Grammar Words

---

## Word 1: Some

### Simple English Meaning

An unspecified amount or number.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kehi

### Use

Usually positive sentences.

### Where to Use

✅ Some books

✅ Some water

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Most negative sentences

### Structure (if applicable)

Some + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ I don't have some money.

✅ I don't have any money.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I bought some books.

Roman Nepali: Maile kehi kitab kine.

Meaning: Unspecified amount.

#### Example 2

English: She needs some help.

Roman Nepali: Unilai kehi sahayog chahincha.

Meaning: Small amount.

#### Example 3

English: We have some food.

Roman Nepali: Hami sanga kehi khana cha.

Meaning: Available quantity.

### Memory Trick

**Some = Usually positive**

---

## Word 2: Any

### Simple English Meaning

An unspecified amount or number.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kunai pani

### Use

Questions and negatives.

### Where to Use

✅ Any money?

✅ Any books?

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Most positive statements

### Structure (if applicable)

Any + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ I have any questions.

✅ I have some questions.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Do you have any money?

Roman Nepali: Timisanga kunai paisa cha?

Meaning: Question.

#### Example 2

English: I don't have any books.

Roman Nepali: Masanga kunai kitab chaina.

Meaning: Negative.

#### Example 3

English: Is there any water?

Roman Nepali: Ke pani cha?

Meaning: Question.

### Memory Trick

**Any = Questions/Negatives**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Some | Kehi | Positive |
| Any | Kunai pani | Questions/Negatives |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I bought ______ fruit.
2. Do you have ______ questions?
3. I don't need ______ help.

Answers:

1. some
2. any
3. any

---

## Quick Revision

* Some = Positive
* Any = Question/Negative

---

# 85. Each / Every

## Type

Confusing Grammar Words

---

## Word 1: Each

### Simple English Meaning

One by one in a group.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Pratyek

### Use

Focus on individuals.

### Where to Use

✅ Each student

✅ Each child

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Very large general groups (often every is better)

### Structure (if applicable)

Each + singular noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Each students.

✅ Each student.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Each student received a book.

Roman Nepali: Pratyek bidyarthile kitab payo.

Meaning: One by one.

#### Example 2

English: Each child got a gift.

Roman Nepali: Pratyek bachhale upahar payo.

Meaning: Individual focus.

#### Example 3

English: Each player tried hard.

Roman Nepali: Pratyek kheladile mehenat garyo.

Meaning: Every individual.

### Memory Trick

**Each = One by one**

---

## Word 2: Every

### Simple English Meaning

All members of a group.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Harek

### Use

Whole group.

### Where to Use

✅ Every day

✅ Every student

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Plural noun directly

### Structure (if applicable)

Every + singular noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Every students.

✅ Every student.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I study every day.

Roman Nepali: Ma harek din padhchu.

Meaning: All days.

#### Example 2

English: Every student passed.

Roman Nepali: Harek bidyarthi pass bhayo.

Meaning: Whole group.

#### Example 3

English: She visits every week.

Roman Nepali: Uni harek hapta aauchhin.

Meaning: Regularly.

### Memory Trick

**Every = Whole group**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Each | Pratyek | Individual focus |
| Every | Harek | Whole group |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. ______ student received a certificate.
2. I exercise ______ day.
3. ______ child got a toy.

Answers:

1. Each
2. every
3. Each

---

## Quick Revision

* Each = Individual
* Every = Whole group

--------


# 86. Either / Neither

## Type

Confusing Grammar Words

---

## Word 1: Either

### Simple English Meaning

One of two people or things.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dui madhye kunai ek

### Use

When there are two choices.

### Where to Use

✅ Either answer

✅ Either road

✅ Either of them

### Where NOT to Use

❌ More than two choices

### Structure (if applicable)

Either + singular noun

Either of + plural noun/pronoun

### Common Mistake

❌ Either three students can come.

✅ Any of the three students can come.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: You can choose either book.

Roman Nepali: Timi dui kitab madhye jun sukai chhanot garna sakchhau.

Meaning: One of two.

#### Example 2

English: Either road leads to the city.

Roman Nepali: Dui madhye jun bato le pani sahar pugauchha.

Meaning: Any one of two.

#### Example 3

English: Either of them can help.

Roman Nepali: Uniharu dui madhye junle pani sahayog garna sakchha.

Meaning: One of two people.

### Memory Trick

**Either = One of two**

---

## Word 2: Neither

### Simple English Meaning

Not one and not the other.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dui madhye kunai pani hoina

### Use

Negative meaning with two choices.

### Where to Use

✅ Neither answer

✅ Neither of them

### Where NOT to Use

❌ More than two choices

### Structure (if applicable)

Neither + singular noun

Neither of + plural noun/pronoun

### Common Mistake

❌ Neither of them are here.

✅ Neither of them is here.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Neither answer is correct.

Roman Nepali: Dui madhye kunai uttar sahi chaina.

Meaning: Both are wrong.

#### Example 2

English: Neither of them came.

Roman Nepali: Uniharu dui madhye kunai pani aayenan.

Meaning: No one came.

#### Example 3

English: Neither road is safe.

Roman Nepali: Dui madhye kunai bato surakshit chaina.

Meaning: Both unsafe.

### Memory Trick

**Neither = Not either**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Either | Dui madhye ek | Positive choice |
| Neither | Dui madhye kunai pani hoina | Negative choice |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. ______ of the two answers is correct.
2. You may choose ______ book.
3. ______ of them attended the class.

Answers:

1. Neither
2. either
3. Neither

---

## Quick Revision

* Either = One of two
* Neither = None of two

---

# 87. Between / Among

## Type

Confusing Preposition

---

## Word 1: Between

### Simple English Meaning

In the middle of two people, things, or groups.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bichma

### Use

Usually with two.

### Where to Use

✅ Between Ram and Hari

✅ Between two houses

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Large group without clear separation

### Structure (if applicable)

Between + A and B

### Common Mistake

❌ Between five students.

✅ Among five students.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The school is between two shops.

Roman Nepali: School dui pasal bichma cha.

Meaning: Middle of two.

#### Example 2

English: Sit between Ram and Hari.

Roman Nepali: Ram ra Hari bichma basa.

Meaning: Between two people.

#### Example 3

English: The agreement was between the two countries.

Roman Nepali: Samjhauta dui desh bich bhayo.

Meaning: Relation of two.

### Memory Trick

**Between = Two**

---

## Word 2: Among

### Simple English Meaning

In the middle of many people or things.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dherai bichma

### Use

Three or more.

### Where to Use

✅ Among students

✅ Among friends

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Exactly two people

### Structure (if applicable)

Among + group

### Common Mistake

❌ Among Ram and Hari.

✅ Between Ram and Hari.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: She was among friends.

Roman Nepali: Uni sathiharu bichma thin.

Meaning: Part of group.

#### Example 2

English: The teacher walked among the students.

Roman Nepali: Shikshak bidyarthiharu bichma hidnu bhayo.

Meaning: Inside group.

#### Example 3

English: He is popular among children.

Roman Nepali: Uni bachhaharu bich lokpriya chan.

Meaning: Within group.

### Memory Trick

**Among = Many**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Between | Bichma | Two |
| Among | Dherai bichma | Three or more |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Sit ______ me and Ram.
2. She is popular ______ students.
3. The ball was ______ two chairs.

Answers:

1. between
2. among
3. between

---

## Quick Revision

* Between = Two
* Among = Many

---

# 88. Beside / Besides

## Type

Look-Alike Word

---

## Word 1: Beside

### Simple English Meaning

Next to.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Chheu ma

### Use

Position.

### Where to Use

✅ Beside the table

✅ Beside me

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Meaning "in addition to"

### Structure (if applicable)

Beside + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Beside English, I study Math.

✅ Besides English, I study Math.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Sit beside me.

Roman Nepali: Mero chheu ma basa.

Meaning: Next to.

#### Example 2

English: The bag is beside the chair.

Roman Nepali: Jhola kursiko chheu ma cha.

Meaning: Nearby position.

#### Example 3

English: She stood beside her mother.

Roman Nepali: Uni aamako chheu ma ubhin.

Meaning: Next to person.

### Memory Trick

**Beside = Side**

---

## Word 2: Besides

### Simple English Meaning

In addition to.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bahek pani

### Use

Extra information.

### Where to Use

✅ Besides English

✅ Besides that

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Physical location

### Structure (if applicable)

Besides + noun/pronoun

### Common Mistake

❌ The chair is besides me.

✅ The chair is beside me.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Besides English, I study Science.

Roman Nepali: English bahek ma Science pani padhchu.

Meaning: In addition.

#### Example 2

English: Besides Ram, Hari came too.

Roman Nepali: Ram bahek Hari pani aayo.

Meaning: Extra person.

#### Example 3

English: Besides that, I agree.

Roman Nepali: Tyo bahek ma sahamat chu.

Meaning: Additional point.

### Memory Trick

**Besides = In addition**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Beside | Chheu ma | Next to |
| Besides | Bahek pani | In addition |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Sit ______ me.
2. ______ English, she knows Nepali.
3. The dog stood ______ its owner.

Answers:

1. beside
2. Besides
3. beside

---

## Quick Revision

* Beside = Next to
* Besides = In addition

---

# 89. Above / Over

## Type

Confusing Preposition

---

## Word 1: Above

### Simple English Meaning

At a higher level.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Mathi

### Use

Higher position without touching.

### Where to Use

✅ Above sea level

✅ Above the door

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Covering something

### Structure (if applicable)

Above + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Put a blanket above me.

✅ Put a blanket over me.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The picture is above the sofa.

Roman Nepali: Tasbir sofako mathi cha.

Meaning: Higher position.

#### Example 2

English: The plane flew above the clouds.

Roman Nepali: Jahaj badal mathi udayo.

Meaning: Higher level.

#### Example 3

English: Temperatures rose above 30°C.

Roman Nepali: Tapakram 30°C bhanda mathi pugyo.

Meaning: Higher than.

### Memory Trick

**Above = Higher position**

---

## Word 2: Over

### Simple English Meaning

Above and sometimes covering or crossing.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Mathi bata

### Use

Movement, covering, or position.

### Where to Use

✅ Over the bridge

✅ Over the table

✅ Over the bed

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Measurement comparison

### Structure (if applicable)

Over + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ The temperature is over the average. (possible, but above is often better)

✅ The temperature is above average.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The bird flew over the house.

Roman Nepali: Chara ghar mathi bata udayo.

Meaning: Crossing above.

#### Example 2

English: Put a blanket over the baby.

Roman Nepali: Bachha mathi kambal rakha.

Meaning: Covering.

#### Example 3

English: We walked over the bridge.

Roman Nepali: Hami pul mathi bata hidyau.

Meaning: Across.

### Memory Trick

**Over = Above + covering/crossing**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Above | Mathi | Higher position |
| Over | Mathi bata | Covering/crossing |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. The fan is ______ my head.
2. Put a cloth ______ the table.
3. The bird flew ______ the river.

Answers:

1. above
2. over
3. over

---

## Quick Revision

* Above = Higher position
* Over = Covering/crossing

---

# 90. Below / Under

## Type

Confusing Preposition

---

## Word 1: Below

### Simple English Meaning

At a lower level.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Tal

### Use

Lower position or amount.

### Where to Use

✅ Below average

✅ Below the picture

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Covered by something

### Structure (if applicable)

Below + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ The cat is below the blanket.

✅ The cat is under the blanket.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Read the text below.

Roman Nepali: Tala ko path padha.

Meaning: Lower position.

#### Example 2

English: The temperature is below zero.

Roman Nepali: Tapakram sunya bhanda tala cha.

Meaning: Lower amount.

#### Example 3

English: The village is below the hill.

Roman Nepali: Gaun pahad tala cha.

Meaning: Lower level.

### Memory Trick

**Below = Lower level**

---

## Word 2: Under

### Simple English Meaning

Beneath and often covered by something.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Muni

### Use

Directly beneath.

### Where to Use

✅ Under the table

✅ Under the blanket

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Numerical comparison

### Structure (if applicable)

Under + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Under average score.

✅ Below average score.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The cat is under the table.

Roman Nepali: Biraalo table muni cha.

Meaning: Directly beneath.

#### Example 2

English: The keys are under the bed.

Roman Nepali: Chabi bed muni cha.

Meaning: Hidden beneath.

#### Example 3

English: The child slept under a blanket.

Roman Nepali: Bachha kambal muni sutyo.

Meaning: Covered.

### Memory Trick

**Under = Beneath/covered**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Below | Tal | Lower level |
| Under | Muni | Beneath/covered |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. The shoes are ______ the bed.
2. The score is ______ average.
3. The village lies ______ the mountain.

Answers:

1. under
2. below
3. below

---

## Quick Revision

* Below = Lower level
* Under = Beneath/covered

------

# 91. In Time / On Time

## Type

Confusing Expression

---

## Word 1: In Time

### Simple English Meaning

Early enough; before it is too late.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Samay mai

### Use

When something happens before a deadline or danger.

### Where to Use

✅ In time to catch the bus

✅ In time for the meeting

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Exact scheduled time

### Structure (if applicable)

In time + to + verb

In time for + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ The train arrived in time at 8:00.

✅ The train arrived on time at 8:00.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: We arrived in time for dinner.

Roman Nepali: Hami khanako lagi samay mai pugyau.

Meaning: Not late.

#### Example 2

English: She reached in time to catch the bus.

Roman Nepali: Uni bus samatna samay mai pughin.

Meaning: Before it left.

#### Example 3

English: The doctor came in time.

Roman Nepali: Doctor samay mai aae.

Meaning: Before it was too late.

### Memory Trick

**In time = Before it is too late**

---

## Word 2: On Time

### Simple English Meaning

Exactly at the planned time.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Thik samay ma

### Use

Schedules and appointments.

### Where to Use

✅ On time for class

✅ On time for work

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Meaning "before it is too late"

### Structure (if applicable)

On time

### Common Mistake

❌ I arrived on time to save him.

✅ I arrived in time to save him.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The train arrived on time.

Roman Nepali: Train thik samay ma aayo.

Meaning: Not early or late.

#### Example 2

English: She is always on time.

Roman Nepali: Uni sadhai thik samay ma aauchhin.

Meaning: Punctual.

#### Example 3

English: Please come on time.

Roman Nepali: Kripaya thik samay ma aau.

Meaning: Follow schedule.

### Memory Trick

**On time = Exact schedule**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| In time | Samay mai | Before too late |
| On time | Thik samay ma | Exact schedule |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. The bus arrived ______.
2. We got there ______ to catch the train.
3. She is always ______ for class.

Answers:

1. on time
2. in time
3. on time

---

## Quick Revision

* In time = Before too late
* On time = Exact schedule

---

# 92. At the End / In the End

## Type

Confusing Expression

---

## Word 1: At the End

### Simple English Meaning

At the final part of something.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Antim bhag ma

### Use

Specific ending point.

### Where to Use

✅ At the end of the road

✅ At the end of the book

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Final result after events

### Structure (if applicable)

At the end of + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ At the end, we won after many problems.

✅ In the end, we won after many problems.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: At the end of the movie, everyone clapped.

Roman Nepali: Filmko antim bhag ma sabai le tali bajaye.

Meaning: Final part.

#### Example 2

English: There is a shop at the end of the street.

Roman Nepali: Sadakko antim tira pasal cha.

Meaning: Physical ending.

#### Example 3

English: Read the questions at the end of the chapter.

Roman Nepali: Adhyayako antimma bhayeka prashna padha.

Meaning: Last section.

### Memory Trick

**At the end = Physical/specific ending**

---

## Word 2: In the End

### Simple English Meaning

Finally; after everything.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Antya ma

### Use

Final result.

### Where to Use

✅ In the end, we succeeded.

✅ In the end, he agreed.

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Physical location

### Structure (if applicable)

In the end + result

### Common Mistake

❌ The school is in the end of the road.

✅ The school is at the end of the road.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: In the end, we won.

Roman Nepali: Antya ma hami jitiyau.

Meaning: Final result.

#### Example 2

English: In the end, he accepted the offer.

Roman Nepali: Antya ma unile prastab swikar gare.

Meaning: After everything.

#### Example 3

English: In the end, everyone was happy.

Roman Nepali: Antya ma sabai khusi bhaye.

Meaning: Final outcome.

### Memory Trick

**In the end = Finally**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| At the end | Antim bhag ma | Specific ending |
| In the end | Antya ma | Final result |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. There is a park ______ the road.
2. ______, they solved the problem.
3. Read the note ______ the page.

Answers:

1. at the end of
2. In the end
3. at the end of

---

## Quick Revision

* At the end = Specific ending
* In the end = Finally

---

Look-Alike Word

#### Example 3

English: His house has three rooms.

Meaning: Building details.

The place where you live and feel comfortable.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Ghar

### Use

Living place, not building focus.

### Where NOT to Use

Go home

Come home

Stay at home

### Common Mistake

#### Example 1

English: I am going home.

Meaning: Feeling of belonging.

### Memory Trick

**Home = Place you live**

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| House | Building | Physical structure |
| Home | Ghar | Living place |

* Home = Living place

Confusing Verb

To lift something up.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

### Use

When a person causes something to go up.

### Where to Use

### Where NOT to Use

### Structure (if applicable)

Raise + object

### Common Mistake

#### Example 1

English: Please raise your hand.

English: They raised the flag.

Meaning: Caused it to go up.

#### Example 3

English: We raised money for charity.

Roman Nepali: Hami le daanko lagi paisa uthayau.

Meaning: Collected.

## Word 2: Rise

### Simple English Meaning

To go up.

### Use

When something goes up by itself.

### Where to Use

✅ The sun rises

### Where NOT to Use

### Structure (if applicable)

Rise (no object)

### Common Mistake

English: The sun rises in the east.

#### Example 2

English: Prices are rising.

Meaning: Increasing.

#### Example 3

English: The balloon rose into the air.

Meaning: Moved upward.

English: Lay the book on the table.

### Structure (if applicable)

Lie + place

#### Example 1

To describe character or nature.

#### Example 1

### Memory Trick

**Be like = Character**

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |

| Be like | Kasto hunu | Character |

Confusing Verb

---

### Where NOT to Use

### Common Mistake

#### Example 2

English: She borrowed some money.

Meaning: Received temporarily.

#### Example 3

English: Can I borrow your pen?

Meaning: Take and return.

### Memory Trick

---

## Word 2: Lend

### Simple English Meaning

To give something temporarily.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Sapati dinu

### Use

Giver's action.

### Where to Use

✅ Lend money

✅ Lend a book

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Taking from someone

### Structure (if applicable)

Lend + object + to

### Common Mistake

❌ I lent a book from Ram.

#### Example 1

English: I lent my book to Hari.

Roman Nepali: Maile Hari lai mero kitab sapati diye.

Meaning: Gave temporarily.

#### Example 2

English: She lent me some money.

Roman Nepali: Unile malai kehi paisa sapati din.

Meaning: Gave temporarily.

#### Example 3

English: Can you lend me a pen?

Roman Nepali: Ke timi malai kalam sapati dina sakchhau?

Meaning: Give for short time.

### Memory Trick

**Lend = Give**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Borrow | Sapati linu | Take temporarily |
| Lend | Sapati dinu | Give temporarily |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Can I ______ your book?
2. She ______ me some money.
3. I ______ a pen from my friend.

2. lent
3. borrowed

---

## Quick Revision

* Borrow = Take
* Lend = Give

Focus on the words spoken.

### Common Mistake

❌ He said me the answer.

✅ He told me the answer.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Please say your name.

Roman Nepali: Kripaya aafno naam bhana.

Meaning: Speak words.

#### Example 2

English: She said hello.

Roman Nepali: Unile namaste bhanin.

Meaning: Spoke greeting.

#### Example 3

English: He said that he was tired.

Roman Nepali: Unile bhane ki uni thakita thiye.

Meaning: Reported speech.

### Memory Trick

**Say = Words**

---

## Word 2: Tell

### Simple English Meaning

To give information to someone.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bataunu

### Use

Focus on the listener.

### Where to Use

✅ Tell me

✅ Tell him

✅ Tell a story

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Without listener (usually)

### Structure (if applicable)

Tell + person + information

### Common Mistake

❌ Tell that you are ready.

✅ Say that you are ready.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Tell me the truth.

Roman Nepali: Malai satya batau.

Meaning: Give information.

#### Example 2

English: She told us a story.

Roman Nepali: Unile hamilai euta katha sunain.

Meaning: Spoke to listeners.

#### Example 3

English: Please tell him the news.

Roman Nepali: Kripaya unilai samachar batau.

Meaning: Inform someone.

### Memory Trick

**Tell = Person**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Say | Bhannu | Focus on words |
| Tell | Bataunu | Focus on listener |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Please ______ me your name.
2. She ______ hello.
3. He ______ me the answer.

Answers:

1. tell
2. said
3. told

---

## Quick Revision

* Say = Words
* Tell = Person

---

# 102. Speak / Talk

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Speak

### Simple English Meaning

To use your voice formally.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bolnu

### Use

Languages and formal communication.

### Where to Use

✅ Speak English

✅ Speak to the manager

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Casual friendly conversation (often talk is better)

### Structure (if applicable)

Speak + language

Speak to/with + person

### Common Mistake

❌ I speak with my friend every evening. (possible but less natural)

✅ I talk with my friend every evening.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: She speaks English well.

Roman Nepali: Uni English ramro bolchhin.

Meaning: Language ability.

#### Example 2

English: May I speak to the manager?

Roman Nepali: Ke ma manager sanga kura garna sakchu?

Meaning: Formal communication.

#### Example 3

English: He spoke at the meeting.

Roman Nepali: Unile baithakma bole.

Meaning: Formal speaking.

### Memory Trick

**Speak = Formal / Language**

---

## Word 2: Talk

### Simple English Meaning

To have a conversation.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Kura garnu

### Use

Informal communication.

### Where to Use

✅ Talk with friends

✅ Talk about sports

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Language ability

### Structure (if applicable)

Talk to/with + person

Talk about + topic

### Common Mistake

❌ I can talk French.

✅ I can speak French.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: We talked for an hour.

Roman Nepali: Hami ek ghanta kura garyau.

Meaning: Conversation.

#### Example 2

English: Let's talk about school.

Roman Nepali: Schoolko barema kura garau.

Meaning: Discussion.

#### Example 3

English: She talked to her friend.

Roman Nepali: Unile aafno sathi sanga kura garin.

Meaning: Casual conversation.

### Memory Trick

**Talk = Conversation**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Speak | Bolnu | Formal/language |
| Talk | Kura garnu | Conversation |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I can ______ English.
2. We ______ about football.
3. May I ______ to the teacher?

Answers:

1. speak
2. talked
3. speak

---

## Quick Revision

* Speak = Language/Formal
* Talk = Conversation

---

# 103. Hear / Listen

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Hear

### Simple English Meaning

To receive sound naturally.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

### Use

No effort needed.

### Where to Use

✅ Hear a noise

### Where NOT to Use

### Common Mistake

❌ Listen a noise.

English: I heard a loud sound.

Meaning: Sound reached ears.

#### Example 2

English: Can you hear me?

Meaning: Receive sound.

#### Example 3

English: We heard the bell.

Meaning: Heard naturally.

### Memory Trick

---

## Word 2: Listen

### Simple English Meaning

To pay attention to sound.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Dhyan diyera sunnu

### Use

Intentional action.

### Where to Use

✅ Listen to music

### Where NOT to Use

### Structure (if applicable)

Listen to + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Listen music.

✅ Listen to music.

#### Example 1

English: Listen to the teacher.

#### Example 2

English: I like to listen to music.

Meaning: Intentional listening.

#### Example 3

English: Please listen carefully.

Meaning: Focus.

### Memory Trick

**Listen = Attention**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Hear | Sunnu | Sound reaches you |
| Listen | Dhyan diyera sunnu | Attention |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I can ______ birds singing.
2. Please ______ to me.
3. We ______ a strange noise.

2. listen

## Quick Revision

* Listen = Pay attention

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. ______ at the board.

3. I can ______ a bird.

* See = Notice
* Watch = Observe long

# 106. Trip / Travel / Journey / Tour

Look-Alike Words

## Word 1: Trip

### Use

### Where NOT to Use

English: He took a trip to Pokhara.

Meaning: Visit to a place.

#### Example 3

English: The trip was enjoyable.

Roman Nepali: Yatra ramailo thiyo.

Meaning: Travel experience.

## Word 2: Travel

### Simple English Meaning

The activity of going from place to place.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Yatra garnu

### Use

General movement.

### Where to Use

✅ Travel abroad

✅ Love to travel

### Where NOT to Use

### Structure (if applicable)

Travel + place

### Common Mistake

❌ I had a good travel.

✅ I had a good trip.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: I love to travel.

Roman Nepali: Malai yatra garn man parchha.

Meaning: General activity.

#### Example 2

English: She travels often.

Roman Nepali: Uni dherai yatra garchhin.

Meaning: Frequent movement.

English: We travel by bus.

Meaning: Transportation.

### Memory Trick

**Travel = Activity**

## Word 3: Journey

The act of travelling from one place to another.

### Use

Focus on the travel itself.

### Where to Use

### Where NOT to Use

### Structure (if applicable)

A journey from A to B

### Common Mistake

❌ We journey tourism places. 

✅ We went on a tour of tourist places.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: The journey took five hours.

Roman Nepali: Yatra panch ghanta lagyo.

Meaning: Travel process.

#### Example 2

English: It was a long journey.

Roman Nepali: Yo lamo yatra thiyo.

Meaning: Travel distance.

#### Example 3

English: The journey was difficult.

Roman Nepali: Yatra garo thiyo.

Meaning: Travel experience.

### Memory Trick

**Journey = Travel process**

---

## Word 4: Tour

### Simple English Meaning

A visit to several places for pleasure.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Bhraman

### Use

Sightseeing.

### Where to Use

✅ City tour

### Where NOT to Use

### Common Mistake

✅ My journey to work takes 20 minutes.

#### Example 1

English: We went on a city tour.

Roman Nepali: Hami sahar bhramanma gayau.

Meaning: Sightseeing.

#### Example 2

English: The guide led the tour.

Roman Nepali: Guidele bhraman chalaye.

Meaning: Organized visit.

#### Example 3

English: They toured Europe.

Roman Nepali: Uniharule Europe bhraman gare.

Meaning: Visited many places.

### Memory Trick

**Tour = Sightseeing**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Trip | Chhoto yatra | Short visit |
| Travel | Yatra garnu | General activity |
| Journey | Yatra | Travel process |
| Tour | Bhraman | Sightseeing |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. I love to ______.
2. We went on a school ______.
3. The ______ lasted eight hours.

Answers:

1. travel
2. trip
3. journey

---

## Quick Revision

* Trip = Short visit
* Travel = Activity
* Journey = Travel process
* Tour = Sightseeing

Meaning: Recalled an event.

### Memory Trick

**Remember = I keep it in my mind**

---

## Word 2: Remind

### Simple English Meaning

To help someone remember something.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Samjhaunu

### Use

When one person helps another remember.

### Where to Use

✅ Remind me

✅ Remind him

✅ Remind us

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Recalling your own memory

### Structure (if applicable)

Remind + person + about/of

Remind + person + to + verb

### Common Mistake

❌ I reminded his name.

✅ I remembered his name.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Please remind me tomorrow.

Roman Nepali: Kripaya malai bholi samjhaideu.

Meaning: Help me remember.

#### Example 2

English: She reminded him to study.

Roman Nepali: Unile unilai padhna samjhaain.

Meaning: Gave a reminder.

#### Example 3

English: This photo reminds me of my childhood.

Roman Nepali: Yo photole malai balyakal samjhaunchha.

Meaning: Brings memory back.

### Memory Trick

**Remind = Help another person remember**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Remember | Samjhinu | Recall yourself |
| Remind | Samjhaunu | Help others remember |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Please ______ me to call Hari.
2. I ______ her birthday.
3. This song ______ me of school days.

Answers:

1. remind
2. remember
3. reminds

---

## Quick Revision

* Remember = Recall
* Remind = Help recall

---

# 112. Discover / Invent

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Discover

### Simple English Meaning

To find something that already exists.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Pattaa lagaunu

### Use

Finding something unknown.

### Where to Use

✅ Discover a place

✅ Discover a fact

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Creating something new

### Structure (if applicable)

Discover + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Edison discovered the light bulb.

✅ Edison invented the light bulb.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Scientists discovered a new planet.

Roman Nepali: Bigyaniharu le naya graha pattaa lagaye.

Meaning: Found something existing.

#### Example 2

English: We discovered the truth.

Roman Nepali: Hami le satya pattaa lagayau.

Meaning: Found information.

#### Example 3

English: They discovered a hidden cave.

Roman Nepali: Uniharule lukeko gufa pattaa lagaye.

Meaning: Found something.

### Memory Trick

**Discover = Find**

---

## Word 2: Invent

### Simple English Meaning

To create something new.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Aabiskar garnu

### Use

Making something that did not exist before.

### Where to Use

✅ Invent a machine

✅ Invent a device

### Where NOT to Use

❌ Finding existing things

### Structure (if applicable)

Invent + noun

### Common Mistake

❌ Columbus invented America.

✅ Columbus discovered America.

### Examples

#### Example 1

English: Edison invented the light bulb.

Roman Nepali: Edisonle bulb aabiskar gare.

Meaning: Created new item.

#### Example 2

English: She invented a useful tool.

Roman Nepali: Unile upayogi aujaar banain.

Meaning: Created something new.

#### Example 3

English: Scientists invent new technologies.

Roman Nepali: Bigyaniharu le naya pravidhi banauchhan.

Meaning: Create innovations.

### Memory Trick

**Invent = Create**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Discover | Pattaa lagaunu | Find existing thing |
| Invent | Aabiskar garnu | Create new thing |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Scientists ______ a new species.
2. Edison ______ the light bulb.
3. We ______ an old temple.

Answers:

1. discovered
2. invented
3. discovered

---

## Quick Revision

* Discover = Find
* Invent = Create

Look-Alike Word

### Where NOT to Use

English: This is a great opportunity.

Something that may happen.

### Use

Probability.

### Where NOT to Use

## Exam Pattern

# 116. Customer / Client

Look-Alike Word

### Use

### Where to Use

### Where NOT to Use

#### Example 1

Used with lawyers, consultants, accountants, designers, agencies, etc.

Meaning: Professional customers.

#### Example 3

Meaning: Service receiver.

### Memory Trick

* Customer = Buyer

# 119. Bring / Take

## Type

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Bring

### Simple English Meaning

To move something toward the speaker.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Lyaunu

### Use

Toward here.

### Where to Use

✅ Bring your book here.

✅ Bring food to me.

### Where NOT to Use

### Structure (if applicable)

Bring + object + here

### Common Mistake

❌ Take your book here.

#### Example 1

English: Please bring your notebook.

Roman Nepali: Kripaya aafno copy lyaau.

Meaning: Move toward speaker.

English: Can you bring some water?

Meaning: Bring here.

#### Example 3

English: She brought flowers for me.

Roman Nepali: Unile malagi phool lyaain.

Meaning: Carried toward person.

### Memory Trick

**Bring = Come toward me**

## Word 2: Take

To move something away from the speaker.

### Roman Nepali Meaning

Lagnu

### Use

Away from here.

### Where to Use

English: Take this bag home.

Meaning: Carried away.

#### Example 3

English: Please take your umbrella.

Roman Nepali: Kripaya aafno chata laga.

Meaning: Move away from current place.

### Memory Trick

**Take = Go away**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Bring | Lyaunu | Toward speaker |
| Take | Lagnu | Away from speaker |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

1. Please ______ your book here.
2. ______ this package to the office.
3. She ______ flowers for me.

2. Take
3. brought

## Quick Revision

* Bring = Toward
* Take = Away

Confusing Verb

---

## Word 1: Rob

### Roman Nepali Meaning

### Use

Focus on the victim.

### Common Mistake

English: The thieves robbed a bank.

English: He was robbed yesterday.

Meaning: Person was victim.

Meaning: Place was victim.

**Rob = Victim**

## Word 2: Steal

Focus on the thing taken.

### Where NOT to Use

#### Example 1

Meaning: Took illegally.

English: The thief stole a bicycle.

Roman Nepali: Chorle cycle choryo.

Meaning: Object taken.

### Memory Trick

**Steal = Thing taken**

---

## Comparison Summary

| Word | Meaning | Use |
|------|---------|------|
| Rob | Lootnu | Victim/person/place |
| Steal | Chornu | Object taken |

---

## Exam Pattern

Fill in the blank:

2. The gang ______ a bank.

## Quick Revision

* Rob = Victim
* Steal = Object taken
